# 03 — Findings Register

*Diagnostic report, Phase 8. Read-only analysis; nothing in this report was fixed.*

> **This register is live and has been amended by every wave since.** The line above
> describes the analysis that produced rows F-01..F-55 on 2026-08-08, not the state of
> the register today. **84 of the 177 rows are closed, and each closed row names the
> commit that closed it** — wave 6b established that evidence from the repository, and
> wave 7 closed the Critical that had been open longest. Two figures that used to sit
> here were of wave 9 and were carried unchanged through eight later waves: `60 of the
> 140` (wave-9 close, `f014e2f`), and *"wave 9 left F-79 as the register's only
> `(scheduled)` row"* — F-135 has carried that marker since wave 10, so there are two.
> Both are corrected rather than deleted, because the drift is itself **F-131**. A row's
> status is the marker in its **Effort** cell; see *"How this register is counted, and
> which rows are closed"* below the totals.

Severity: **Critical** = incorrect scientific output, data loss, or security ·
**High** = blocks maintenance or peer review · **Medium** · **Low** = cosmetic.
Effort: **XS** ≤ 30 min · **S** ≤ half a day · **M** 1–3 days · **L** > 3 days.

---

## The register

| ID | Sev | Category | Finding | Evidence | Impact | Suggested fix | Effort |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **F-01** | **Critical** | correctness | **The LLM cache key omits the criterion's content.** Only the criterion *id* (`cid`) enters the key, but the prompt carries the criterion's `type`, `operator`, `target`, `what`, `label`, and `threshold`. | `plugins/_common/llm_client.py:397-414` (key = `prompt_version\|model\|cid\|a_id\|text_hash\|trunc_chars`); prompt content built at `plugins/06_el/screen.py:434-443` and serialised at `plugins/06_el/prompt.py:42-51`; lookup at `screen.py:457` | A researcher who refines the wording of `IC-1` — the most common edit during a live review — and re-runs EL/IL receives **the previous criterion's answers from cache, verbatim, with no warning**. The evidence JSON then shows quotes and decisions produced against different criterion text. UI reports a normal `cache_hits=N`. | Hash the serialised `crit_pack` into the cache key. Will invalidate `tests/golden/{el,il}_cache_v3.1.0.json` and require a re-capture — make that trade-off explicitly. **Fixed in `34fa37a`, goldens re-keyed in `c8d2fb3`:** the key is now a SHA-256 over a stable JSON serialisation of `{prompt_version, model, temperature, rendered_prompt}`, so criterion wording, record text, targets and truncation reach the key without being enumerated — `_cache_key`'s own docstring records the principle, *"Enumeration was itself the bug."* The predicted re-capture was avoided: `c8d2fb3` re-keyed the existing EL/IL cache goldens offline, values byte-identical, so the decision CSVs did not move. The trade-off this row asked to be made explicitly is recorded in `CHANGELOG.md`. Three later rows sit in the same artefact and are open — F-89 (endpoint absent from the key), F-101 (`batch_size` invisible), F-102 (a truncation step-down keying on the un-stepped prompt). | S (+ golden re-capture) (done) |
| **F-02** | **Critical** | correctness | **Cancelling a run silently truncates the output with no marker.** `if cancel_event.is_set(): break` exits the row loop mid-corpus; the partial `full_rows`/`survivors` are returned as if complete. | `plugins/_common/runner.py:101,119`; `plugins/06_el/screen.py:430,511`; `plugins/07_il/screen.py:432,513` | A user who cancels and then exports gets a bundle that is **indistinguishable from a complete run over a smaller corpus**. Records never evaluated simply do not appear in the FULL report. In a screening tool this is a missing-data failure with scientific consequences. | Return a `cancelled: bool` alongside the results; refuse export (or stamp `manifest.pipeline.history[].cancelled = true` and label the reports) when set. **Fixed in `9812f6d`:** all four engines return a `cancelled` flag; one shared gate, `plugins/_common/bundle.py::_export_block_reason`, refuses export from all six stage shells; EH/IH additionally disable both export buttons and label the run `CANCELLED — partial: N of M records screened`; and `_export_next_bundle_zip` stamps `manifest.pipeline.history[].cancelled` as defence in depth. Both halves of the suggested fix landed. | S (done) |
| **F-03** | **Critical** | correctness | **`data/input_errors.csv` uses three incompatible schemas and is destroyed by EL/IL.** Harmoniser writes `record_number,reason,observed_len,expected_len,raw`; EH/IH write `record_index_ex_header,reason,raw_record`; EL/IL write `reason,row_json`. The only reader expects the second. | Writers: `plugins/03_harmoniser/exporters.py:160-166`, `plugins/_common/exporters.py:45`, `plugins/06_el/ui.py:1053`. Reader: `plugins/_common/parser.py:318-338`. EL skip-set that prevents carry-forward: `plugins/06_el/ui.py:1003`. **Proven:** feeding the Harmoniser's file to the reader returns `[]` | Every citation dropped at ingestion loses its provenance at the first hop (`04_eh/ui.py:460` then reports "0 rows"). At EL/IL the file is **deleted from the bundle** unless EL itself skipped rows. The audit trail for excluded-as-malformed records does not survive the pipeline. | Pick one schema, put the writer in `_common`, make EL/IL append rather than overwrite, and add a round-trip test. **Fixed in `a39b49d`, completed in `c928432`:** `plugins/_common/input_errors.py` became the single schema (`stage,record_number,reason,observed_len,expected_len,raw` — the Harmoniser's, widened), the single reader and the single writer; every stage merges the incoming file rather than overwriting or deleting it; and `tests/test_input_errors.py` pins the four-hop round trip. **Both commits are load-bearing:** `a39b49d` left standing the third writer this row's own evidence cell names — the EL/IL "Export input_errors.csv…" buttons — which were retired only by `c928432`, logged there as F-74. A row citing `a39b49d` alone would credit it with a closure it did not complete. | S (done) |
| **F-04** | **High** | correctness | **IL treats a criterion with an empty `type` cell as an exclusion criterion**, inverting its polarity. | `plugins/07_il/screen.py:261` — `ctype = … or "exclude"` — inside the IL stage parser; polarity applied at `07_il/screen.py:553-571` | A hand-edited or third-party `criteria_harmonized.csv` with a blank `type` on an IL row makes the LLM's `meet` verdict **exclude** the record instead of including it. Silent, and exactly backwards. | Default to `"include"` in IL (and keep `"exclude"` in EL), or better: reject a criterion with no `type` and surface a warning. **Fixed in `f925625`, refined in `906423a`:** a criterion whose `type` cell is blank or unrecognised is now rejected with a warning rather than defaulted, in both EL and IL — the row's "or better" branch. `906423a` made the warning name the 1-based spreadsheet row and state the remedy. Rejecting rather than defaulting to the stage's own polarity is the safer direction: a missing criterion is visible in the criteria panel, an inverted one is not. | XS (done) |
| **F-05** | **High** | correctness / documentation | **Stages 06 and 07 never verify or refresh SHA-256.** The string `sha` appears **zero** times in `06_el/ui.py`, `07_il/ui.py`, and both `standalone.py`. Both stages *do* overwrite `data/current.csv`. | `plugins/06_el/ui.py:1028` writes the file; no `sha256` map update anywhere in the module. Contrast `plugins/_common/bundle.py:208-215`. Claim: `README.md:95` | The bundle leaving EL carries a manifest digest that **no longer matches the file it names** — the manifest actively asserts something false — and nothing downstream checks. The README's "any modification … is detectable" is false for precisely the two stages that carry the LLM decisions. | Route EL/IL bundle export through a shared writer that refreshes `sha256`; add load-time verification. Soften the README claim to "corruption-detecting, not tamper-proof". **Fixed in `080aa3e` (code half) and `65ae997` (documentation half):** EL and IL now export through the shared `plugins/_common/bundle.py::_write_llm_stage_bundle`, which refreshes the `sha256` map, and verify it on load; the README claim was restored in full rather than softened, because all four stages now verify. One nuance for a careful reader: EH/IH's load-time check is narrower than EL/IL's, verifying `data/current.csv` and the criteria table rather than every member — the row's own claim, that a modification to the record set is detectable, holds. | S (done) |
| **F-06** | **High** | correctness | **`majority_vote` returns `"uncertain"`, which is not in `CANONICAL_DECISIONS = ("yes","no","unsure")`.** Combined with F-07 this silently corrupts Cohen's kappa on any tied overlap item. | `tools/eval_ingest.py:600-606` vs `:85`; consumed at `:635` | Any tie between the three raters produces a pair the confusion matrix drops but `n` still counts. **Verified not triggered in the committed data** (0 ties in 45 overlap items; reported `N` equals every matrix total). Dormant, but a re-run with a different sample or rater set would silently mis-state the validation study's headline statistic. | Change `"uncertain"` → `"unsure"` (two occurrences). **Fixed in `336c2c2`:** `majority_vote` returns the canonical `"unsure"` on a tie, so no out-of-vocabulary label can reach the confusion matrix. Closed together with F-07, which is the other half of the same statistic. | XS (done) |
| **F-07** | **High** | correctness | **`cohen_kappa` divides by `len(pairs)` while `_confusion_matrix` silently drops out-of-vocabulary pairs.** | `tools/eval_ingest.py:512` (`n = len(pairs)`) vs `:496-498` (`if a in idx and b in idx`) | **Demonstrated:** adding 10 out-of-vocabulary pairs to a clean 50-pair set moves kappa from 0.400000 to 0.361702 with no error. Both `p_o` and `p_e` are deflated. | Raise on an unknown label, or compute `n` from the matrix total and report the drop count. **Fixed in `336c2c2`:** an unrecognised rater label now raises instead of being dropped from the matrix while still counting in the denominator, so `p_o` and `p_e` can no longer be silently deflated. Closed together with F-06. | XS (done) |
| **F-08** | **High** | correctness | **The API-key dialog blocks the documented local-provider workflow.** It requires `startswith("sk-") and len >= 20`, and it cannot be skipped. | `metascreener/api_key_dialog.py:112`; `metascreener/main.py:62,76-91`. Documentation it contradicts: `README.md:314,318,322` (`OPENAI_API_KEY=ollama`, `=llama-cpp`) | The Ollama / llama.cpp / vLLM paths — a full README section, and a likely reviewer probe since it is the project's answer to "does this require a paid API?" — **cannot be exercised through the GUI at all**. | Accept any non-empty key; downgrade the `sk-` check to a non-blocking hint. Optionally skip the prompt when a valid key is already in the environment. **Fixed in `9fc1eaa`:** the dialog accepts any non-empty key; the `sk-` rule is a grey advisory rather than a refusal. The optional "skip the prompt when a key is already in the environment" was not implemented. **This row's Impact claim is not thereby satisfied, and F-91 records why:** the key rule was the *second* barrier to the documented local-provider workflow, and the first still stands — no widget anywhere binds the endpoint, so a GUI-only user can supply the placeholder key and still cannot point the client away from OpenAI. `CHANGELOG.md`'s entry for this row over-states the closure on that point. | XS (done) |
| **F-09** | **High** → **Medium** *(measured; see `04_frozen_build.md`)* | packaging | **The PyInstaller builds shipped without `langdetect`, `rapidfuzz` and `pytesseract`** — three of the six predicted, not all six. `pandas`, `openpyxl` and `PIL` *were* present, by accident: `collect_all('openai')` pulls `openai._extras.pandas_proxy` → `pandas` → `pandas.io.excel._openpyxl` → `openpyxl` → `openpyxl.drawing.image` → `PIL`. **The predicted symptom did not occur.** All seven plugin modules import and all seven tabs are added, because every heavy dependency is behind a `try/except` feature flag or a lazy in-function import. | Measured on a real build (PyInstaller 6.15.0, Windows 11): `PYZ-00.pyz` contained 2960 pure modules with `langdetect`/`rapidfuzz`/`pytesseract` at 0 and `pandas` 280, `openpyxl` 176, `PIL` 80. Guards at `plugins/02_references_of_x/core.py:34-46` (`PANDAS_OK`, `FUZZY_OK`), `services.py:44-49` (`_LANGDETECT_OK`), and the lazy import at `plugins/01_reference_extractor/plugin.py:36`. `discover()` has no `try/except`, so a failed plugin import would abort startup with a traceback; none appeared in any run of either build. | Downgraded from High because nothing crashes and no tab is lost. The real cost is **silent feature degradation**: `FUZZY_OK=False` disables fuzzy title matching in a reference-matching tool, `_LANGDETECT_OK=False` makes language detection return `""` for everything, and OCR is unavailable — none of it surfaced to the user (see F-66). Second cost: three plugins' dependencies rested on an implementation detail of `openai`, and would have vanished together if that package dropped its extras proxy. | **Fixed in `d8d8a96`:** `hookspath=['.']` *and* `'plugins'` in `hiddenimports` — see F-40 for why the first alone does nothing. Pure modules 2960 → 3054; `langdetect` 0→10, `rapidfuzz` 0→35, `pytesseract` 0→2, `plugins` 0→47. | S (done) |

| **F-12** | **High** *(narrowed in wave 6 — headline was stale, mechanism was wrong; see below)* | testing | **~~The entire LLM interaction path is 0% covered.~~ Narrowed: the LLM *error and salvage* paths have no coverage, and the transport layer has none at all.** The happy path of `plugins/_common/llm_client.py::run_m1_llm_for_criterion` — batching, the batch loop, a successful call, the parse loop, `return out` — **does** execute under the suite. What never executes is every branch that fires when a model or an endpoint misbehaves: 429 handling, adaptive batch splitting, truncation step-down, terminal-failure back-fill, every `_parse_llm_json_array` fallback, and every response-normalisation coercion. `::_openai_client_for` and `::_has_openai_key` never execute at all. | `06_llm_integration.md` C-16 and §A11.2–A11.3, which supply both corrections. The unset key is real — `tests/test_el_regression.py::_el_to_csv` does pop `OPENAI_API_KEY` — but it is **not what short-circuits the call**: measured with the key deliberately left set, the replay path still makes zero client constructions and zero key checks, because the guard `if c.operator == "llm" and to_call:` in `plugins/06_el/screen.py::run_el_screen` is false when the cache is complete. The transport gap is the sharper half: nothing anywhere asserts which endpoint the client resolves to (F-92). | Unit-test the error branches against a stub client object — no network needed; the function only calls `client.chat.completions.create`. **Generalise the test double first:** its keyword-only `create` signature fails on the first added parameter, which makes it a hidden pin on the request shape (F-107). | **F-22** | **Medium** | correctness | **The quote check is not Unicode-normalised or case-folded**, so encoding variation inflates the flag rate. **Refined in wave 6:** the gate normalises *whitespace only*, and the proposed NFKC fix below is necessary but **not sufficient**. | `plugins/_common/llm_client.py::_quote_in_text` — exact match, then whitespace-collapsed match only. **Wave-6 measurements (`06_llm_integration.md` C-23 and §A7.3):** case, NFC/NFD accents, curly versus straight quotes, en/em dash versus hyphen, `ﬁ`/`ﬂ` ligatures, U+200B/200C/200D, U+FEFF and U+00AD **all** fail the gate. NFKC alone rescues the NBSP and NFD cases and leaves the rest: verified in Python that `unicodedata.normalize("NFKC", …)` does **not** remove U+200B/200C/200D/U+FEFF/U+00AD and does **not** fold case. Note `normalize_dashes` already exists in plugin 01 — the capability is in the repository and simply absent from the gate. | Fails *closed* (records go to human review), so it is safe — but a corpus with mixed NFC/NFD or smart punctuation flags records for reasons unrelated to the criterion, silently degrading the tool's headline reduction figure. Wave 6 sharpens the population: the inflation is **systematically worse for weaker and local models, and for non-English and PDF-derived corpora**, which are precisely the configurations the local-provider work would introduce. | NFKC **plus** stripping the zero-width/soft-hyphen set **plus** `casefold()` **plus** a dash/quote fold, on both sides before the second comparison. | **F-25** | **Medium** | correctness | **No `max_tokens` and no timeout on the OpenAI client.** | `plugins/_common/llm_client.py::_openai_client_for` (the client) and `::run_m1_llm_for_criterion` (the call). **Magnitude corrected in wave 6 (`06_llm_integration.md` C-21):** the original cell said a hung endpoint blocks a worker for "the SDK default (600 s)". Read from installed SDK source, the SDK sets `DEFAULT_TIMEOUT = httpx.Timeout(timeout=600, connect=5.0)` **and** `DEFAULT_MAX_RETRIES = 2`, and the repository configures neither — so **one `_call_once` can spend 3 × 600 s = 30 minutes** while the worker thread holds and the UI shows a stale progress event. | A batch whose response exceeds the model's output limit is truncated mid-JSON; `_parse_llm_json_array` returns `[]`; **the whole batch is back-filled as uncertain** with no detection and no retry, because truncation does not raise. A hung endpoint blocks a worker for up to half an hour. **Telemetry consequence of the same omission:** because the SDK retries twice beneath the application's own ladder, **every logged "batch failed" line under-reports the request count threefold** — which matters for anyone reasoning about cost or about a rate limit, and is the number a user would quote when asking why a run was expensive. | Set an explicit per-request `timeout=` **and** an explicit `max_retries=` chosen in awareness of the application's own retry ladder (which fixes the log line as a side effect); detect `finish_reason == "length"` and halve the batch as the 429 path already does — `finish_reason` is currently never read at all (F-122). Cross-ref F-94 (the classifier that makes every timeout terminal on first sight), F-122, F-87. | S |
| **F-26** | **Medium** → **High** *(corrected during the wave 2 fix — see below)* | correctness | **Cancelling did not merely discard LLM results already paid for; it overwrote received answers with fabricated ones.** `_check_cancel()` raised a bare `RuntimeError("Cancelled")`, which unwound past `return out` — that much was in the original row. **Correction:** the post-call check at `llm_client.py:231` sits *inside* the per-batch retry `try`, so the generic `except Exception` handler at `:323` caught the cancellation, matched neither the rate-limit nor the context-length branch, and fell through to its "final failure for this batch" path — writing every item in the batch out as `decision="uncertain", confidence=0.0, valid_quote=False, error="Cancelled"`. | `plugins/_common/llm_client.py:172-176,206,231` (the raise sites) and `:323-375` (the handler that swallowed it and fabricated the replacements) | The original row understated this as wasted spend. The real impact is **evidential**: a batch whose API call had already succeeded and returned real decisions had those decisions replaced by manufactured `uncertain` verdicts, which then flow into `el_evidence_json` / the row-detail modal and into the cache if the run is later resumed — indistinguishable from a genuine model non-answer except for the `error` field, which nothing surfaces. A user pressing Cancel could therefore *change screening evidence*, not just lose it. | Raise a dedicated exception type so the retry handler re-raises rather than swallowing it, catch it at the batch loop, return the partial `out`, and drop the post-call check entirely — the answer is already paid for by then, so the thing worth skipping is the *next* batch, which the check at the top of the loop already does. **Fixed in `9812f6d`:** a dedicated `plugins/_common/llm_client.py::_Cancelled` replaces the bare `RuntimeError`; the per-batch retry block guards it with an explicit `except _Cancelled: raise` *ahead of* the generic handler, so the fabricated-`uncertain` overwrite this row's correction identified can no longer happen; and the batch loop returns the partial results already paid for. *(Note on this row's own history: `f2cc47c` is a register edit, not a code change — it raised the severity and wrote the "Correction:" clause 27 minutes after `9812f6d` had already fixed the fuller defect.)* | XS (done) |

| **F-34** | **High** *(raised from Medium — see below)* | correctness | **A stage with zero enabled criteria reports a successful clean pass.** Every record is assigned `PASS_CLEAN` and the run summary shows all records surviving, with the fact that no screening happened confined to a warning line. | `plugins/_common/parser.py:373` and `plugins/06_el/screen.py:323` / `07_il/screen.py:325` emit the warning; `plugins/_common/runner.py:99-115` and `plugins/06_el/screen.py:386-404` then assign `PASS_CLEAN` to every row. **Measured** on a bundle built from `tests/golden/criteria_harmonized_v3.1.0.csv`: `counts: {'OUT': 0, 'PASS_CLEAN': 85, 'PASS_FLAGGED': 0}`, `survivors: 85 of 85`. | A stage that did nothing is indistinguishable from a stage that ran correctly and excluded nothing — and `PASS_CLEAN` is the *stronger* of the two survivor labels, meaning "every criterion was met", which is precisely what did not happen. **Raised to High because F-04 added a second and more likely route to it:** rejecting a criterion whose `type` cell is blank means one malformed cell can now empty a stage, where previously the stage would at least have run (with inverted polarity). A single-criterion stage — which EL is, in the demonstration corpus, after `EC-3` — goes from "wrong answer" to "no answer that looks like a right answer". See F-04. | A stage with zero enabled criteria must not report success: block the run or label the outcome distinctly (e.g. `NOT_SCREENED`), and require explicit acknowledgement before export. The records still pass through to the next stage: not having screened them is no reason to drop them. Compounded by the one-underscore confusion with `docs/`. | Document the rule in `docs_/README.md`; consider renaming to `samples/` after acceptance (touches `pyproject.toml:75`, README ×4, `docs/index.md`, `docs/usage.md`). **Fixed in `f0ea616`** (wave 5, D1/D3): renamed to top-level `samples/`, flat layout; the `.gitignore` block deleted rather than retargeted — nothing writes into the tree at runtime, so the blanket rule only ever hid accidental manual additions; full reference sweep per the docs_/docs diagnostic's §6(i) list, with history (CHANGELOG, `docs/internal/**`) left as written; guard test `TestNoStaleSampleFolderReferences` pins the old name out of the published docs. | S (done) |
| **F-55** | **Low** | testing | **EH/IH do not detect duplicate `local_id`; EL/IL do.** | `plugins/06_el/screen.py:213-215` drops duplicates; `plugins/_common/parser.py:270-315` has no such check | A corpus with duplicate ids screens cleanly through stages 04–05 and silently loses rows at stage 06. | Add the check to `_parse_csv_tolerant_text`. **Fixed in `4e8bdc7`:** the EH/IH corpus parser skips repeated `local_id` values, matching what EL already did, and records them as `duplicate_local_id:<id>` so the drop reaches the audit trail rather than happening silently between stages. | XS (done) |

| **F-59** | **Medium** | correctness | **Plugin 02's pause/resume hooks guard on methods the View does not define**, so they would no-op even if fired correctly. `RefXPlugin.on_hide` checks `hasattr(self.view, "request_pause")` and `on_show` checks `request_resume`; `ReferencesOfXView` defines neither. | `plugins/02_references_of_x/plugin.py:71-91` (the guards) vs `plugins/02_references_of_x/ui.py:388` `ReferencesOfXView`, whose only pause/resume-adjacent method is `on_stop` (line 942). The `_request_pause`/`_request_resume` pair lives on `_BaseModal` (line 129), `ResolveModal` (335) and `FetchModal` (360) — modal helper classes the plugin never reaches. | This is a **second, independent** reason the pause/resume feature is dead, on top of F-18: fixing the dispatch alone changes nothing, because the guard fails and both hooks fall through silently. Long resolve/fetch runs keep consuming CPU and API quota while their tab is off-screen. Note the contrast with `on_unload`, whose guard on `view.on_stop` **does** resolve — that hook works now that F-18 fires it. | Decide whether the feature is wanted. If yes, expose `request_pause`/`request_resume` on `ReferencesOfXView` delegating to the active modal's `_RunControl` (`ui.py:104`), and wire `on_hide` to tab-leave in `_on_tab_changed`. If no, delete `on_hide`/`on_show` from the plugin — they are not in the `BasePlugin` contract. | S |
| **F-60** | **Medium** | testing | **`_load_plugins` itself is untested.** The lifecycle tests exercise `resolve_plugin_entrypoint` and `notify_plugin` against fake modules; the loop that actually registers plugins is covered only by an AST assertion that the source contains a `self._plugins.append` call. | `metascreener/main.py:228-245`; tests at `tests/test_plugin_lifecycle.py` (`TestResolveReturnsTheInstance`, `TestNotifyPlugin` use fakes; `TestPluginsListIsPopulated` parses the source rather than running it) | A structural check cannot catch an ordering or alignment bug — e.g. appending after `nb.add` on one branch, or skipping the append when `frame` is falsy-but-not-None — which would silently de-align `_plugins` from the notebook's tab indices and make `_on_tab_changed` fire the wrong plugin's hook. The method cannot be exercised without a Tk root, which is why it was never covered. | Fold into the headless View smoke test proposed as action 7 in the top-ten list: build a real `Tk()`, run `_load_plugins` over the seven real plugins, assert `len(self._plugins) == nb.index("end")` and that entry *i* corresponds to tab *i*. Skip when no display is available. | S |

| **F-61** | **Low** *(investigated as a suspected Critical — see the note below the register)* | hygiene | **`run_screen` did not filter criteria on `enabled`.** The EH/IH runner took `criteria_report.criteria` wholesale, so any disabled criterion in the report it was handed would have been evaluated and could have excluded records. **Unreachable through the application:** the EH/IH criteria loader drops disabled rows before building the report, and EL/IL filter at run, so no disabled criterion has ever reached an evaluation loop. | Defect: `plugins/_common/runner.py:90` before `dc622fa` (`crits = criteria_report.criteria`). Why it is unreachable: `plugins/_common/parser.py:390-392` (`enabled = _truthy(...)`; `if not enabled: continue`) for EH/IH, and `plugins/06_el/screen.py:410` / `plugins/07_il/screen.py:412` (`crits = [c for c in criteria_report.criteria if c.enabled]`) for EL/IL — the latter present since before wave 0. The toggle itself is Plugin 03's criteria table (`plugins/03_harmoniser/ui.py:695-699`), which writes `enabled=0` to `criteria_harmonized.csv` (`plugins/03_harmoniser/exporters.py:87`). Verified by loading a two-row table with one row disabled: only the enabled `cid` reaches the report. | **No user-facing impact, and no released version can have produced a wrong exclusion from this.** It is a latent gap only: a caller that constructs a `CriteriaLoadReport` in code — as `tests/test_not_screened.py` does — would have got silent evaluation of disabled criteria, and the same omission meant "zero *enabled* criteria" could not be detected in EH/IH at all, which is how it surfaced. | Fixed in `dc622fa` as a prerequisite to F-34: filter on `enabled`, and key `crit_impacts` over all criteria so the criteria table keeps a zeroed row for a disabled one. Defence in depth — the parser remains the primary filter. | XS (done) |

| **F-62** | **Medium** | hygiene | **The `used` field is misnamed and misleads anyone auditing a cache or an evidence trail.** It reads as "this evidence was used in the decision". It actually means "a parseable response object was received for this `a_id`" — pure provenance, with no bearing on whether the entry influenced the outcome. The two come apart exactly where it matters: an entry can be `used=true` and still be discarded by the evidence gate, which is precisely the `used=true, valid_quote=false` combination that prompted this finding. | Set `True` at one site only, inside the response-parsing loop: `plugins/_common/llm_client.py:319`. Set `False` at `:335` (the model omitted this `a_id` from its answer — back-fill), `:408` (batch failed terminally), and `plugins/06_el/screen.py:559` / `07_il/screen.py:561` (non-llm operator). Re-exported into the evidence JSON at `06_el/screen.py:639` / `07_il/screen.py:641`. Observed misreading: a real archived bundle with 38 `used=true, valid_quote=false` entries was read as evidence that unverified quotes were being acted on; they were not — see F-63's sibling analysis and the gate at `plugins/06_el/screen.py::run_el_screen`. | Anyone reading a cache file or an evidence trail — the two artefacts a reviewer is most likely to open — will conclude that a decision was acted on when it was discarded, or that coverage is influence. It cost one full investigation cycle here, by the author of the tool. | Rename to `response_received` (or `model_answered`). **Migration cost is not trivial and must be planned:** the key lives inside the *value* dicts of `cache/{EL,IL}_cache.jsonl` and `tests/golden/{el,il}_cache_v3.1.0.json`, and it is also copied into `{el,il}_evidence_json`, which is a column of `tests/golden/{el,il}_filtered_v3.1.0.csv`. So a rename changes the filtered goldens byte-for-byte — unlike the F-01 re-key, this cannot be done as a pure relabelling of keys. Cheapest safe path: accept both names on read for one release (`ev.get("response_received", ev.get("used", False))`), write only the new one, and regenerate the filtered goldens by scripted field-rename with a byte-diff review confining the change to that one JSON key. | S |
| **F-63** | **Medium** | correctness | **Evidence spans are accepted unvalidated and can point nowhere.** `span = obj.get("span", None)` is checked only for being a list of exactly two ints; it is never compared against the quote, against the located position of the quote, or against the length of the source field. The quote itself *is* validated (`_quote_in_text`), so the span is the one piece of the evidence trail with no integrity check at all. | `plugins/_common/llm_client.py:309-311` (the only validation: `isinstance(span, list) and len(span) == 2 and all(isinstance(x, int) …)`), stored at `:324`, re-exported at `06_el/screen.py:638` / `07_il/screen.py:640`. **Real evidence:** an archived run of 2026-05-07 wrote only three distinct spans across 170 entries — `(0,100)` ×85, `(0,14)` ×50, `(0,0)` ×35 — where `(0,100)` is identical across an entire 85-record criterion sweep, and `(0,0)` co-occurs with the empty quotes that fail `_quote_in_text` at `llm_client.py:96`. **Confirmed against the committed goldens in wave 6** (`06_llm_integration.md` C-22, the span half — a straight duplicate of this row carrying new evidence): **169 of 170 EL and 77 of 84 IL golden entries have a span that does not locate the quote.** So the defect is not confined to one archived run; it is the state of the shipped fixtures, and therefore of the published dataset (F-98). §B8 adds one consequence this row could not have anticipated: **under more than one model the span becomes the field a reader would use to *compare* evidence quality between engines**, and every such comparison would measure noise. | The span is offered to the user as the location of the supporting evidence, in the row-detail modal and in the exported `{el,il}_evidence_json`. In a provenance tool a span that points nowhere is worse than no span: it invites a reviewer to verify a claim at coordinates that were never checked, and a boilerplate span survives into the archived bundle looking like a measurement. Note the harm is bounded — the *quote* gate still governs the decision (F-62 evidence), so a bogus span cannot change an outcome. | Either compute the span from the validated quote (after `_quote_in_text` succeeds, take `field_text.find(quote)` and emit that, or `None` when the match was only whitespace-normalised), or drop the field entirely and let the quote stand alone. Computing it is preferable: it is the only version that is checkable. **Same golden cost as F-62** — spans are inside `{el,il}_evidence_json`, so any change moves `{el,il}_filtered_v3.1.0.csv`. | S |
| **F-64** | **Medium** | correctness | **The evidence JSON emits two incompatible shapes for the same `UNCERTAIN` status.** The LLM branch writes nine keys including `quote_valid`, `confidence` and `threshold`; the non-llm-operator branch writes only `{"status": "UNCERTAIN", "note": …}`. A consumer parsing the evidence trail therefore meets `UNCERTAIN` entries where the diagnostic keys are simply absent, with no signal that a different shape is in play. | Full shape at `06_el/screen.py:630-640` / `07_il/screen.py:632-642`; the truncated shape at `06_el/screen.py:592` / `07_il/screen.py:594` (`{"status":"UNCERTAIN", "note":"non-llm operator in EL stage"}`); a third shape, `{"status":"MISSING"}`, at `06_el/screen.py:586`. **Measured** on `tests/golden/il_filtered_v3.1.0.csv`: of 168 IL evidence entries, 30 `UNCERTAIN` are diagnosable as below-threshold and 7 as failed-quote, while **84 — every `IC-5` entry — carry none of the diagnostic keys**, because `IC-5` uses the `contains` operator and never reaches the LLM branch. | Two consequences. An auditor writing one parser for the evidence trail gets `KeyError` or a silent `None` on half the IL entries. And the larger fact the shape hides: a deterministic (`contains`) criterion contributes nothing but an `UNCERTAIN` in the LLM stages — `IC-5` was never evaluated on any record, it merely blocked `PASS_CLEAN` for all 84 — which is invisible unless you already know what the `note` means. | Emit one shape for all branches, with the criterion-level keys always present and `None` where not applicable, and promote `note` to a first-class `uncertain_reason` field taking a small closed vocabulary (`failed_quote`, `below_threshold`, `model_uncertain`, `non_llm_operator`, `missing_target`). Then a single parse answers "why was this uncertain?" for every entry. Same golden cost as F-62 and F-63. **Extended in wave 6** (`06_llm_integration.md` C-22, the `error` half): the evidence dict is built from a fixed nine-key list that **omits `error` entirely**, so a record whose criterion was never evaluated *because the call failed* is visually identical in the export to one the model genuinely could not judge. The vocabulary proposed above has no member for that case — add `call_failed` (or a first-class `status: "ERROR"`) and carry the `error` string, or the reason field will be complete in form and incomplete in fact. This also gives F-87 somewhere to record what it currently caches silently. Cross-ref F-109 (there is no `STATUSES` constant anywhere, which is why the two shapes diverged unnoticed). | S |

| **F-65** | **High** | correctness | **A deterministic criterion assigned to an LLM stage is never evaluated, and the Harmoniser's own inference assigns them there.** EL and IL execute `operator == "llm"` and nothing else: every other operator is marked `UNCERTAIN` without being evaluated. This is not specific to `contains` — it is every non-`llm` operator at an LLM stage. The mirror case exists too (EH/IH return `UNKNOWN` for `llm`), but that direction is at least surfaced in the stage's detail modal, and this one is not. | The fall-through: `06_el/screen.py:555-559` and `:590-594`, `07_il/screen.py:557-561` and `:592-596` — `if c.operator != "llm": uncertain.append(...)`, evidence `{"status":"UNCERTAIN","note":"non-llm operator in IL stage"}`. The heuristic counterpart: `_common/evaluator.py:87-90`, which implements `equals, contains, regex, in_list, not_in, gte, lte, between` and returns `UNKNOWN` for `llm`. **How it is reached in normal use:** `03_harmoniser/inference.py:215-250`, branch 6 ("Simple text search in title/abstract/keywords"), sets `operator = "contains"` and then, at `:249`, explicitly `stage = "IL" if crit_type == "include" else "EL"`. It is the **only** deterministic branch that routes to an LLM stage; branches 1–5 all set `IH`/`EH` — **corrected in wave 13b: the function carries exactly six numbered branches, so the original "branches 1–5 and 7–9" named three that do not exist. [measured]** at `4a33d45` by enumerating every `# n)` comment and every `operator =` / `stage =` assignment inside `_infer_criterion_details`; the substance of the claim is unaffected, branch 6 is still the only one routing to an LLM stage. Its trigger list is broad — `["title","abstract","keywords","keyword","mention","contains","must include","include term"]` — so any criterion label merely *mentioning* those words lands there. Verified: `_infer_criterion_details("include", <IC-5's label>, cols)` returns `stage=IL, operator=contains`; `_validate_row` on that row returns **no error and no warning** (`inference.py:256-300` checks stage and operator separately and never cross-checks them). **Measured on the committed golden:** `IC-5` is `UNCERTAIN` ×84 with that note, `il_met_ids` takes only the values `''` (41) and `'IC-1'` (43) — `IC-5` never appears as met for any record — and `PASS_CLEAN` is 0 (`REVIEW` 80, `OUT` 4). | Two consequences. **The published demonstration ran with seven effective criteria, not the eight its criteria table declares** — `IC-5` has never been applied to any record in any run. And because an `UNCERTAIN` criterion also blocks `PASS_CLEAN` (`07_il/screen.py:645`), the mere presence of a non-`llm` criterion makes the stage structurally incapable of reporting a clean pass: every survivor is `REVIEW`. The only trace is the `note` key inside the truncated evidence shape — which is precisely the shape **F-64** records as unparseable by a consumer expecting the nine-key form. Direction of harm is safe (nothing is wrongly excluded; the criterion is absent and everything falls to human review) which is why this is High and not Critical. | **Route it back, and validate.** The real fix is `inference.py:249`: a `contains` rule is a deterministic metadata rule and belongs at `IH`/`EH`, exactly as branches 3 and 4 already route `contains` (**wave 13b:** "branches 4 and 7", same stale enumeration). Then `_eval_criterion` executes it with existing tested code and no new evaluator is needed. Add `_validate_row` cross-validation (error when the operator is not executable by the assigned stage) as the net, so a hand-authored table cannot reintroduce it silently. **Reject** the third option — implementing deterministic operators inside EL/IL duplicates `_common/evaluator.py` into both stages, which is F-14's twinning problem again, and changes what the stages mean. **This fix changes screening outcomes and must not be done casually:** measured, evaluating `IC-5` as a strict inclusion criterion at IL takes the demonstration corpus from 80 survivors to **13** — 71 of 84 records contain none of "training", "vocational", "workplace". Whether that criterion is usable as a hard filter at all is a human judgement, not a code change. Own wave. | M |

| **F-66** | **Medium** | correctness | **Optional-dependency degradation is silent and unreportable.** Plugin 02 sets `PANDAS_OK`, `FUZZY_OK`, `REQ_OK` and `_LANGDETECT_OK` when its optional imports fail, and Plugin 01 guards `pytesseract` the same way. Nothing ever surfaces those flags. A build missing an optional dependency is indistinguishable, to the user, from a complete one. | `plugins/02_references_of_x/core.py:34-52` (`PANDAS_OK`, `FUZZY_OK`, `REQ_OK`), `plugins/02_references_of_x/services.py:44-49` (`_LANGDETECT_OK`, consumed at `:921-926` where `_heuristic_lang` returns `""`), `plugins/01_reference_extractor/original/prisma_citations_ai_v3_1.py:54`. Found while measuring the frozen build, which shipped without `rapidfuzz`, `langdetect` and `pytesseract` for exactly this reason and gave no indication (`04_frozen_build.md`). | This is the *actual* failure mode of a mis-specified build, and it is quieter than the missing tabs F-09 predicted. A reference-matching tool with `FUZZY_OK=False` performs no fuzzy title matching, so records that should have matched simply do not — the same class of defect as F-34, an operation that did not happen being reported as one that did. Affects a researcher's results, not just their convenience. | Surface the flags: a startup log line naming every optional dependency that failed to import, and a visible marker in the plugin's own UI for the features it has disabled. Consider promoting `rapidfuzz` from optional to required — it is in `requirements.txt` already, so the `try/except` claims an optionality the project does not otherwise offer. **Not closed, despite a `test(F-66):` commit.** `b586230` added `tests/test_frozen_build_spec.py`, a static assertion that both PyInstaller specs can reach every third-party package imported under `plugins/`. That guards the F-09/F-40 packaging fix against a spec edit; it operates on a different layer from this row, which is about whether a *user* learns that a dependency is missing. Verified at HEAD: `PANDAS_OK`, `FUZZY_OK`, `REQ_OK` and `_LANGDETECT_OK` are still set in bare `try/except` blocks, every consumer is a silent branch, `plugins/02_references_of_x/ui.py` never references them, and no startup log line anywhere enumerates a failed optional import. The second suggestion — promote `rapidfuzz` from optional to required — is also undone: it is a hard entry in `requirements.txt` and still wrapped in `try/except`. F-106 additionally records that the very spec check `b586230` added cannot distinguish a missing DLL from a missing module. | S |

Sites: `plugins/_common/exporters.py:84`, `plugins/_common/input_errors.py:114`, `plugins/06_el/ui.py:984`, `plugins/07_il/ui.py:1232`. Contrast the default `"\r\n"` writers (`_common/bundle.py:458`, `03_harmoniser/exporters.py:138`), which quote it correctly | The audit file that records which citations were dropped can be written in a form its own reader cannot read. Not reachable on the EH/IH report path only because `parser.py:237` strips every CR first (F-76) — an accident of ordering, not a guard, and it disappears the moment a bundle is opened directly at EL. | Pass `lineterminator="\r\n"` (or omit the argument) at every site. **Scheduling:** the `input_errors` chain is wave 4a; the `_common/exporters.py:84` report-writer site is subsumed by F-79 in wave 4b; the two button sites disappear under F-74. **Fixed in `267007d`:** the `input_errors` writer takes the csv default `\r\n`, which quotes the lone CR; round-trip pinned under hostile content. **Residual:** the EH/IH report-writer site at `_common/exporters.py:84` keeps its `\n` terminator until F-79 (wave 4b, golden-touching); the two button sites were deleted by F-74 as predicted. | XS (done) — closed in 4a; the `_common/exporters.py` report-writer site is carried by F-79 |

| **F-69** | **High** | correctness | **The four stage sheets of `reports/ScreenA_Report.xlsx` have never contained data.** The header constant and the row builder were written against different schemas and intersect in 2 of 7 column names, so five columns can never be populated. | `05_report_production.md` candidate 3 and §A/Stage 07. `CONTRACT_STAGE_SHEET_COLS` (`plugins/07_il/plugin.py:56-59`) vs the keys returned by `_extract_contract_stage_row` (`plugins/07_il/ui.py:322-349`); `ws.append([row_obj.get(c, "") for c in …])` (`ui.py:437`) silently yields `""`. **Observed:** every data cell on the EH/IH/EL/IL sheets read back `None`, bar one | `ScreenA_Report.xlsx` is the pipeline's final deliverable and the artefact a reviewer is most likely to open. | **F-70** | **High** | correctness | **The FINAL sheet carries no metadata for any record excluded before IL.** `_load_master_rows` looks for `data/A.csv`, `data/original.csv`, `data/aggregate.csv` and then falls through to `data/current.csv` — and nothing in the pipeline ever writes the first three, so the "master list" is always the terminal survivor set. | `05_report_production.md` candidate 4 and §A/Stage 07. `plugins/07_il/ui.py:298-315`; the Harmoniser writes only `data/current.csv` (`plugins/03_harmoniser/bundle.py:98`). **Observed:** 3 of 6 records had blank title/abstract/keywords on the sheet that is meant to describe the whole corpus | The FINAL sheet's *outcomes* are correct — they are read from the earlier stages' `*_FULL.csv` — so the sheet lists every record with the right verdict and no way to tell which record it is. | **F-86** | **Critical** | correctness / data loss | **Cross-batch answer substitution can export a fabricated exclusion.** `idx_map` is built from the whole `items` list *before* batching, so the acceptance guard `if not a_id or a_id not in idx_map: continue` admits an `a_id` belonging to any other batch. The quote is then validated against *that* record's real text, so `valid_quote` is True and the evidence gate passes. The verdict that excluded the record was produced by a call whose prompt **did not contain that record**. | `06_llm_integration.md` C-1 and §B5.2. `plugins/_common/llm_client.py::run_m1_llm_for_criterion` — `idx_map` construction, the acceptance guard, and the unguarded parse-loop write `out[(a_id, cid)] = {...}`. **Reproduced independently: `OUT = 3/6` with `used: true, quote_valid: true`.** Three aggravations, each measured: it **fires at `batch_size = 1`**, because `idx_map` is built independently of batching; the **backward route is unconditional** — a later batch naming an earlier batch's id destroys an already-correct verdict with no omission required; and it is **persistent**, because the write-back caches the fabricated verdict under the substituted record's own legitimate key, so a later run replays the exclusion with 0 API calls and a normal `cache_hits=N`. | The only finding in the set that can **remove a record from a systematic review on evidence belonging to a different record**, and then reproduce that removal offline for ever. It defeats the project's central safety property (§"What is genuinely good here": every malformed response degrades to *flagged*, never to *acted on*) because nothing here is malformed — the response is well-formed and the quote is genuine, merely of the wrong record. `a_id` drift on a large batch is a characteristic weak-model failure, so the trigger rate rises under exactly the local-model configurations the README invites. | Scope the acceptance guard to the current batch, and guard the parse-loop write the way the back-fill is already guarded. Then add the test this property has never had. **Do this before any local-model work** — C-8/F-93's triggers and this row's trigger are the same populations. **Fixed in `3f37f17`.** The acceptance map is built per batch, inside the loop and per attempt, and supplies both the acceptance guard and the quote-validation text, so neither can reach a record the call did not send; the parse-loop write is guarded the way the back-fill already was, and the first answer for an id wins. Both routes were reproduced before the fix and the persistence half with them — a second run replayed the exclusion from cache at **0 API calls**. `batch_size = 1` confirmed from source and pinned by test. **Two refinements the row's own text does not carry** (`../FIX_WAVE_7_CACHE.md`): the forward route needs the owning batch to *complete and omit* the record — its terminal-failure arm is a second unconditional overwrite that would have destroyed the fabrication — and `items` at both call sites is `to_call`, the criterion's uncached subset, so the substitutable population shrank as the cache warmed. 14 tests in `tests/test_cross_batch_substitution.py`, 8 red before. | S (done) |
| **F-87** | **High** | correctness / integrity | **API failures, refusals and empty responses are written into the persistent cache as verdicts and served for ever.** The write-back loop merges **every** entry of the result map with no filter on `used` or `error`, so a transient 500, a timeout, an auth blip or a refusal is negatively cached under a key that matches on every later run. `ev.setdefault("used", True)` does not repair it. | `06_llm_integration.md` C-2. `plugins/_common/llm_client.py::run_m1_llm_for_criterion` (the terminal-failure back-fill) and the stage write-back in `plugins/06_el/screen.py::run_el_screen` / `plugins/07_il/screen.py::run_il_screen`. The `error` string — including raw SDK exception text — is serialised into the exported bundle. Companion route, measured at `OUT = 2/3`: a duplicate `local_id` collapses two rows onto one verdict and can exclude a record whose own text lacks the quote. | **A network blip becomes a permanent verdict**, indistinguishable from a genuine model non-answer, and re-running does not clear it — the user's remedy (re-run) is precisely the action the cache defeats. Direction of harm is safe (`uncertain` → flagged), so this costs review effort and truthfulness of the trail rather than a wrong exclusion. **The duplicate-id companion is unreachable through the application** and is recorded for the same reason as F-61: `plugins/_common/parser.py` dedupes since F-55 and EL/IL's `_load_bundle` dedupes independently, so only a caller constructing a `ParseReport` directly reaches it. | Refuse to cache any entry carrying `error`, or any with `used=False`. Move the duplicate-id guard from the loader into the engine so the safety property is carried by the gate rather than by the caller. Cross-ref F-33 (unreported cache-line corruption — the same artefact, the opposite direction). **Fixed in `d98d625`.** One shared predicate, `plugins/_common/llm_client.py::_is_cacheable_evidence`, gates both stages' writes: `error` by key presence (it is set from `str(e)`, and an exception with no message stringifies to `""`), `used` by explicit `True`. Write-side only — entries arriving in `cache_in` are carried through untouched, because silently dropping a user's accumulated cache would be its own data loss **and would move the goldens**. The duplicate-id companion is now guarded in both engines, which withhold an ambiguous `local_id` from the LLM entirely rather than asking and discarding, and degrade its rows to UNCERTAIN; the loader dedup stays. Reproduced at `OUT = 2/3` in both stages before the fix, and a failed run's cache was shown to survive a clean re-run at 0 API calls. **Residual, recorded here rather than fixed:** a cache captured before this commit keeps whatever it already holds, and the read path still serves it. 28 tests in `tests/test_negative_caching.py`, 20 red before. | S (done) |
| **F-88** | **High** | provenance / scientific integrity | **No artefact records which model, provider, temperature or prompt version produced a decision.** Across `plugins/` and `metascreener/` the dict key `"model"` occurs exactly once, inside the hashed-and-discarded JSON of the cache key. Not the manifest, not the history entry, not the cache value, not the evidence JSON, not any report column. **A bundle cannot be attributed to a model after the fact.** | `06_llm_integration.md` C-3, §A9.1–A9.3 and §B8.1. `plugins/03_harmoniser/exporters.py::_build_manifest` writes eleven top-level keys, none of them a model; `plugins/_common/bundle.py::_write_llm_stage_bundle` is **never passed** a model or a prompt version by `plugins/06_el/ui.py::ELView._export_next_bundle`, and its history entry is the seven keys `{stage, ran_at, counts, survivors_rows, out_rows_full, cancelled, not_screened}`. **Amended in wave 8 part 2:** the history entry is now **eight** keys, not seven — `d82470e` added `llm`, the run-level failure report. That is a *counting* field and changes nothing about this row: it records how many records were answered, failed or rejected, and still names no model, provider, temperature or prompt version. Tier 1 remains entirely undone. | This is the gap between what the tool claims to be — an auditable, reproducible screening pipeline — and what its own primary artefact records. It makes the FAQ's advice to "pin to a prompt version explicitly in your bundle manifest" unperformable, and it is what forces the published validation study to rest on a field inside a test fixture (F-96). **Graduates F-82 rather than duplicating it:** F-82 is a symmetry defect (EL/IL omit three fields EH/IH stamp) and its fix is verified to leave this untouched — stamping `created_at`, `created_by` and `derived_from.zip_name` into the LLM writer changes `created_by` from `screen_a_ih_plugin` to `screen_a_il_plugin` and records no engine. | Two tiers per §B8.1. **Tier 1 is additive and covered by no golden, so it costs no re-capture**: record model, resolved endpoint, temperature and prompt version in `manifest.pipeline.history[]` at the moment the stage runs. Tier 2 (per-decision attribution inside the evidence JSON) carries the F-62/F-63/F-64 re-capture cost and belongs with them. Blocks F-123 (the documentation currently claims tier 1 already exists). **Wave 9 note:** tier 1 writes into the *manifest* of future runs and is unaffected, but any tier that writes into the **archived** goldens now carries §B4.6's constraint explicitly — a field can be added offline only if its value for the archived run is **known**, and wave 9's re-key had to *infer* even the capture endpoint (F-128). Wave 9 also discharged part of tier 1's motivation incidentally: the endpoint is now in the cache key, so a bundle's cache is at least *partitioned* by provider even though the manifest still does not name one. **Tier 1 of two fixed in `97886f3`; the row stays open.** The documentation claims it falsified were corrected in `97886f3` and `03acf06`. **Tier 1:** Each LLM stage's `manifest.pipeline.history[]` entry now carries a `provenance` block — model, resolved endpoint, temperature, prompt version, truncation limit, batch size — built by `plugins/_common/llm_client.py::llm_provenance` and populated inside `run_el_screen`/`run_il_screen` at the point the endpoint is resolved, which is the only place all six are simultaneously true *of this run*: the endpoint is never returned to the UI, and the model and temperature the UI holds are live widget values editable between the run and the export. **One artefact, argued:** the evidence JSON is tier 2 and carries the F-62/F-63/F-64 re-capture cost; the cache *value* would move the cache goldens and is **redundant**, since F-89 made the cache *key* a hash over the provenance itself, so a value restating it could only agree with its own key; a report column would carry a per-run constant down every row, which is F-69's shape. **Per run — and the cached-decision case, which is the one that matters, is answered rather than conceded:** the key hashes `{prompt_version, model, endpoint, temperature, rendered_prompt}`, so a cached entry *cannot* be served into a run whose provenance differs in any of them, and `trunc_chars` reaches the key through the bytes it removes. The per-run record is therefore a claim about every decision in the stage's output, not only about the calls this run made. `batch_size` is the honest residual — recorded but not keyed, because the key renders a batch of one — and is pinned by test rather than left as a comment. **Six fields rather than four,** because of the wave-8 asymmetry: a negative `trunc_chars` empties fields instead of shortening them and leaves the run report byte-identical to a healthy one, so provenance naming only the engine would inherit the ambiguity between *the model answered* and *the model was shown something*. Transport is a key inside the run report, not a ninth tuple position, as `run_el_screen`'s own docstring requires and `::run_outcome`'s was already written for. Additive throughout: omitted rather than zero-filled when a stage consults no model, old bundles load unchanged and gain no invented provenance, **no golden moved**. 32 tests in `tests/test_provenance.py`. **Tier 2 is not closed and is not re-filed:** it belongs with F-62/F-63/F-64's re-capture as this cell already says, and F-135 owns the per-*call* half, so a third row would be the register disagreeing with itself.| S (tier 1) |

Whoever reads this row next must not implement its suggested fix as written.** The original suggestion, preserved for the record: *"Add `samples/** binary` and `docs/data/** binary`; record `core.autocrlf` in the capture tool's `_invocation` from the next capture forward."* Cross-ref F-128 (the same envelope should also record the SDK version). **Half one — `docs/data/** binary` is too broad and would break a test that currently passes.** Wave 13a established that this row is cause 2 of the red CI, and opened no new row for it: this one already names `docs/data/eval_results_v1.csv` as `text: auto` and already proposes a fix, so a wave-13a row would have been the eighth duplicate this project has found. What this row could not have known is that F-98 later added `tests/test_study_input_freeze.py::TestTheFrozenInputReproducesThePublishedResults`, which compares those same three artefacts byte-for-byte: a fresh Windows clone checks them out CRLF while `tools/eval_ingest.py` regenerates them LF on **every** platform (it opens with an empty `newline=` and pins `lineterminator` to LF), so the test fails in any fresh clone and passes in a working tree that predates the rule — **measured, `3 failed, 9 passed` against `12 passed`**. `docs/data/eval_summary_v1.txt` is LF in the index and CRLF in a Windows working tree, and it is byte-identical on every platform only because `::write_summary_text` uses `Path.write_text` with no `newline=`, which is platform-native and so matches whatever the checkout produced; pinning it to LF while its writer emits CRLF on Windows inverts the failure instead of removing it. **Three globs were measured, not one:** no rule gives `3 failed`, `docs/data/*` gives `1 failed` (on `eval_summary_v1.txt`, inverting exactly as predicted), `docs/data/*.csv` gives `12 passed`. The scoping is load-bearing twice over: `*` does not cross `/` in a gitattributes pattern, so the existing `study_input` and `wave12_local_runs` rules are neither subsumed nor duplicated, and `docs/data/grids/partition_manifest.csv` stays `text: auto` deliberately — it is an *input* to the reproduction, never a compared output. The fix was also checked for blindness rather than only for green: flipping one byte of `docs/data/eval_decisions_v1.csv` in a fresh clone still fails the byte-identity test, and flipping one byte of `docs/data/study_input/el_filtered_v3.1.0.csv` still fails the digest test. **Half two — `samples/** binary` is the exact inverse of what is needed and would break four cells that pass today.** This was cause 3, masked by the `httpx` failure until F-164's fix unmasked it on all twelve non-Windows cells (CI run `31618249170`: 4 green, 12 red, on this one test). `tests/test_wave12_measurement_freeze.py::TestTheOmittedBundleMembersAreAccountedFor` hashes `samples/20260122_1654_aggregate.csv` live against the `data/original.csv` digest recorded in **both** `runB_llama32_manifest.json` and `runC_qwen25_manifest.json` — runA records none. Those manifests hold `b36c3cbb…`, the file **as CRLF**, because the wave-12 runs were made on Windows against the checkout-converted copy; the index holds LF, which hashes to `311442a0…`. **`binary` pins the *index* bytes, so it would make Windows compute `311442a0…` too and break the four cells that currently pass.** The rule that landed is `samples/20260122_1654_aggregate.csv text eol=crlf`, which makes every platform check out the bytes the manifests were computed from — **measured in both arms: a Linux-config clone goes `311442a0…` fail → `b36c3cbb…` pass, and a Windows-config clone stays `b36c3cbb…` pass, full suite `1600 passed, 7 skipped` either side.** Scoped to the one file whose bytes an external manifest pins, derived by enumerating every `sha256` key in all three manifests rather than assumed; the other three files under `samples/` are hashed nowhere. **No frozen manifest was touched** — the manifests are evidence of what the runs actually consumed, so the checkout was made to match them rather than the reverse. Both new rules are now guarded by `tests/test_wave12_measurement_freeze.py::TestTheProtectedSetsAreDeclaredInGitattributes`, which derives the pinned sample set from the manifests instead of restating it. Cross-ref F-164 (the other cause of the same outage), F-165 (why none of the three was noticed for two days). | XS |
| **F-100** | **High** | scientific validity | **The prompt tells the model the confidence threshold it must clear, and the observed confidences cluster immediately above it.** The criterion pack's `threshold` is serialised into the user message; the gate then applies `confidence >= threshold`. | `06_llm_integration.md` C-15 and §A7.5. `plugins/06_el/prompt.py` / `plugins/07_il/prompt.py` (the serialised pack) versus the `usable = …` gate in `plugins/06_el/screen.py::run_el_screen`. **Measured:** 141/170 EL confidences are exactly 0.9 against a threshold of 0.60; every value above the bar is in {0.7…0.95} and every value below in {0.1…0.4}, **with a gap straddling 0.60**. | Telling a model the bar and then treating its self-reported number as an independent gate is a circularity, and no section of the documentation names it. It bears directly on the interpretation of the published quote-validity and agreement figures, because the confidence half of the gate is not measuring what a reader would assume it measures. The bimodal distribution with a gap at the threshold is the signature you would predict from the circularity, which is why this is filed as a validity defect rather than a stylistic one. | Either remove `threshold` from the criterion pack and re-capture, **or** state in `docs/llm-evaluation.md` that the confidence gate is not independent of the prompt. The second is free and honest; the first is better science and carries the F-98 re-capture cost. Cross-ref F-21 (the quote half of the same gate). | S (+ re-capture) or XS (prose) |

| **F-112** | **Medium** | correctness | **Plugin 01 calls `messagebox.showerror` from a worker thread**, while every success-path update in the same function is correctly marshalled through `self.after`. | `06_llm_integration.md` C-29 (adjacent item). ~~`plugins/01_reference_extractor/plugin.py`~~ — the error path versus the `self.after` calls beside it. **Coordinate corrected in wave 8:** `plugins/01_reference_extractor/plugin.py` contains **zero** `messagebox` calls and zero `self.after` calls — it is 61 lines holding `TAB_TITLE`, `make_plugin` and `ReferenceExtractorEmbedded::build_tab`, plus a module-scope `import tkinter.messagebox as messagebox` that nothing uses. That dead import is the likeliest source of the mis-citation. The defect is one directory down, at `plugins/01_reference_extractor/original/prisma_citations_ai_v3_1.py::PrismaAIV3View.on_extract`, in the nested `work()` closure: nine marshalled `self.after(0, …)` on the success path against one bare `messagebox.showerror("Extraction error", str(e))` in the `except`. The `m=str(e)` binding is **required, not stylistic** — PEP 3110 compiles `except Exception as e:` with an implicit `finally: del e`, and the callback runs later on the main loop, so a bare closure would raise `NameError` inside an `after` callback: a silent traceback in the windowed frozen build with the real message lost. **The test is static, and deliberately so.** Observing this at run time means importing a module with unguarded top-level `import fitz` and `from PIL import …`, which would pass here and fail on any machine without them; and `tests/conftest.py`'s `tkinter` mock is thread-agnostic, so it records the call where real Tk raises. `tests/test_worker_thread_tk_safety.py` walks every `threading.Thread(target=…)` closure in `plugins/` and fails on any `messagebox` call not lexically inside an `after(…)` argument — pinning the property rather than the instance. All four are one defect: a third answer to a question `llm_readiness` already answers. `_llm_available` is **removed rather than aliased**, on session A's argument for removing `has_key=`; what remains is `_sdk_importable`, which answers only the question that module can. | S (done) |

Held at Medium, not High: after F-104 there is no remaining *in-code* path that drops a member while its digest survives, so the live triggers are external — a hand-edited zip, a partial extract-and-rezip, a truncated transfer, or the next conditionally-written member. The write is guarded `(a_id, cid) in out -> continue`, matching the omission back-fill twelve lines above; records with no verdict still get an entry, so the guard cannot turn into a leak. And `plugins/_common/llm_client.py::_guarded` wraps the two caller-supplied reporting callbacks once, at the top of the function, so a failure in `progress()` or `log()` cannot reach the retry handler — rebinding the two names rather than editing a dozen call sites means a reporting call added later is covered without anyone having to remember. Cross-ref F-116 (`.env` is the only persistence lever), F-91 (`OPENAI_BASE_URL` has no GUI surface, so this file is the only place it can live), F-127, F-129. **Fixed in `37dd61c` (wave 9), landed here rather than in wave 11 because it can silently undo what wave 9 exists to make meaningful.** Three rules, three different failures: *absent is not unreadable* — a missing `.env` is created, one that raises on read is refused and left byte-for-byte intact; *the write is atomic* — sibling temporary plus `os.replace`, because `Path.write_text` truncates at open and a write interrupted by a full disk is this row's own harm shape through another door, so guarding only the read would have left it open, and `.gitignore` now covers `.env.tmp` so a crash cannot strand the user's key in a committable file; *silence is not success* — `::EnvSaveResult(ok, message)` replaces the bare `None` and `::MetaScreenerApp._prompt_api_key_always` surfaces a failure in a dialog. **Deliberately left for wave 11, and named in the docstring so they are inherited rather than rediscovered:** the save filter matches the literal prefix while `_load_env_file` splits on the first `=` and strips, so `OPENAI_API_KEY = x` and `export OPENAI_API_KEY=x` survive it and win on reload (first occurrence wins), making the newly saved key dead on arrival; a UTF-8 BOM hides the line identically; the value is written unescaped, so an interior newline splits it; and `_load_env_file` still swallows the same read failures, which is how a user reaches the destructive save at all. One residue recorded rather than silently changed: the temporary inherits default permissions, exactly as the pre-existing code created `.env` — not a regression, and tightening it is wave 11's call. **Amended in `c4c30dc`:** an adversarial review of the wave found that the first version of this fix introduced **three regressions of its own**, all in the atomic write, and all now fixed with tests. It replaced a symlinked `.env` with a regular file, left the target stale, and returned `ok=True` — *this row's own defect class, reintroduced by this row's fix* (reproduced, then fixed by resolving `os.path.realpath` first). It widened permissions, because the truncating write it replaced preserved a `chmod 600` on the existing inode while a fresh temporary is born at `0o666 & ~umask` — the mode is now copied from the target, and the brief's earlier claim that this was "not a regression" is corrected. And its constant `.env.tmp` let two instances clobber each other's temporary, one reporting success having written the *other's* key; the name now carries a pid and a random suffix. One consequence is accepted rather than fixed: an atomic write needs permission on the **directory** where truncation needed it only on the file, so a read-only install root with a writable pre-provisioned `.env` now fails — but reports it, and names the path that actually failed. | XS (done) |

The accept/reject decision is therefore taken on a string the application then does not use. | `metascreener/api_key_dialog.py::ApiKeyDialog._on_save` versus `::validate_api_key`, both calling `::sanitize_api_key`. **Measured:** entry `'" x "'` is validated as `'x'` and stored as `' x '`; `self.value` then reaches `os.environ["OPENAI_API_KEY"]` and, with "Remember" ticked, `.env` — so the key sent to the endpoint carries whitespace the validator never saw. | Narrow, but on the axis this project keeps being bitten on: the value checked is not the value used. A padded key is refused by the endpoint with a 401, which today surfaces as a terminal batch failure and a full corpus of manufactured non-answers (F-93), pointing the user at everything except the space. **The reverse route does not exist, and establishing that is half this row's value:** no accepted value can be whitespace-only, because `sanitize` of all-whitespace is empty — which is why F-111's "can only ever render `✓`" claim is *correct*, and this row is the record that it was checked rather than assumed. | Sanitize once, at the boundary, and validate and store the same string. The AST test forbids re-introducing the pre-sanitize. **This row understates it, and the correction matters more than the fix it names:** the dialog the row is written against, `ApiKeyDialog`, was made **unreachable** by wave 11 session B, which replaced the launch modal with `metascreener/provider_dialog.py::ProviderDialog`. That dialog stored `var_key.get().strip()` — whitespace only, **no quote handling at all** — so the same harm arrived by a simpler route on the only live path. Measured: entry `'"sk-a-real-key"'` was stored verbatim, quotes included, and refused by the endpoint with a 401 that surfaces as a terminal batch failure and a corpus of manufactured non-answers (F-93). Both dialogs now share one function; two definitions of what a key is would be F-117's shape applied to the value rather than to the predicate. `_probe` still strips rather than sanitizes, deliberately: it sends the key as a bearer token and never persists it. | XS (done) |
| **F-141** | **High** | documentation / process | **Wave 7's three closures never reached `CHANGELOG.md`, and one of them is the register's last Critical.** F-86, F-87 and F-104 are annotated in their rows and written up in the wave brief, and appear in no user-facing document at all. | `CHANGELOG.md` `[Unreleased]` — searching it for `F-86`, `F-87` or `F-104` returns nothing, while the same section carries a hand-written **"If you produced results with an earlier version, read this first"** enumeration whose entire purpose is this class of disclosure, ordered by how likely each defect was to change which records were included or excluded. The two halves have now failed in both directions, which is what makes this a process row and not just three missing bullets. **High**, on the register's own bar — it blocks a past user from re-checking their own screening output, which is peer review in the sense that bar means. Not Critical: the defects themselves are fixed, and what is missing is the disclosure. | Add all three to `[Unreleased]`: F-86 belongs in the "read this first" list with an explicit re-check condition, F-87 and F-104 in `### Fixed`. Then make the changelog entry part of a wave's close-out checklist, next to the register annotation, so the convention stops depending on whether a brief happens to name it. **Deliberately opened rather than fixed in wave 8:** the wording of a disclosure about fabricated exclusions in already-published reviews is the coordinator's call, not a mechanical edit, and this wave's brief does not scope `CHANGELOG.md`. Cross-ref F-131 (the same hand-maintained-bookkeeping shape, one document over), F-86, F-87, F-104 (all closed), F-30, F-123. **Fixed in `ba68b39`.** Five entries, not three: waves 8 and 9 closed further output-changing defects after this row was written, so **F-90** and **F-143** join F-86, F-87 and F-104. F-86 is **item 1**, the list being ordered by likelihood of having changed which records were included or excluded — which forced a renumbering to 1..18, done programmatically with the item bodies moved verbatim and only the leading number rewritten, and with the three cross-references that name an item by number repaired alongside. Two things the entry does that a normal changelog entry does not. It says **what to do**: a new preamble paragraph states that the remedy in every case is to re-run, because these defects produced plausible artefacts rather than broken ones — nothing to detect, no migration — and because F-89's re-key is what makes re-running *sufficient*. And it says **what cannot be determined**, in a closing paragraph. **The brief's own figure for that paragraph was wrong and was corrected rather than published:** it specified *253 of 254 entries cleared, one structurally undecidable*, which conflates two populations. `../FIX_WAVE_7_CACHE.md` lines 335–364 give **175** cleared by quote uniqueness plus **9** that could not have been substitutions, and **70 undecidable**, at entry level; and separately **4 of the 5 record-removing verdicts** cleared, with one — `A452`/`IC-1`, quote *"Computer science"* — undecidable. The `_invocation` envelope (`batch_size`, `model`, `trunc_chars`) exists **only in the golden capture format, not in the shipped `.jsonl`**, and even it records no code version — F-96's "a field inside a test fixture" again. | **F-149** | **Medium** | correctness | **The provider is probed once, at launch, and never again — so a server that goes away and comes back leaves every LLM tab dead until the application is restarted.** | **Reported from use:** the maintainer's Ollama server stopped, the tab read "Unreachable" with the buttons disabled, he restarted the server, and the tab still read "Unreachable" until he relaunched metaScreener. There is a second, sharper route: `_effective_endpoint` prefers the live widget value, so typing a new endpoint into a tab makes `last_known` return `None` -> `NOT_CHECKED` -> "Checking…" with Run disabled, **and nothing will ever resolve it**, because nothing re-probes. `provider_detect.py:130-132` acknowledges this in a comment ("would block forever if nothing ever probed it"). | Give the user a way to ask again. **Fixed in `be711d0`:** a shared `plugins/_common/widgets.py::RecheckButton`, placed beside the readiness indicator in all three LLM tabs — which is where the user is looking when it says "Unreachable" — with a tooltip stating that the provider is checked once at startup. Ollama never found his RTX 3060 and ran the model entirely on the CPU. The fourth is the load-bearing one: a server that is not Ollama has no such route, and Ollama unloads idle models, so "cannot say" is the ordinary answer and must never be rendered as "CPU" — telling a user with a working GPU that they are on the CPU would send them to reinstall drivers they never needed to touch, which is D5's harm shape. An **absent** `size_vram` on an older build reads as unknown rather than as zero (F-68). Never raises, because it is reached from a pre-run path and a reporting nicety may not block a run. The URL derivation is shared with `model_pull.pull_url`, asserted, because `/api/ps` lives on Ollama's native surface rather than the OpenAI-compatible one. Surfaced in the pre-run confirmation (F-151), which is *before* the four hours rather than after. **The run is not gated on it**: CPU is slow, not wrong. With no measurement the text says there is no basis for one, because a plausible seconds-per-request constant is exactly how F-125 came to describe a cost wrong by two to three orders of magnitude. The rate is a session mean, not the last call, so one cold load does not become the estimate; and it is in memory only, because a rate measured against a since-restarted server that has now found its GPU is worse than none. The confirmation sits **after** the readiness check (no point confirming a run that cannot start) and **before** any control enters its running state (declining leaves the tab as it was). It also carries the compute mode (F-150) and, when in force, flag-only (F-145), because that changes what the run *means* rather than what it costs. Also demonstrated by the freeze: the six recorded fields are not even the full set of things that differed between runs A and B — the criteria **table** differed too, in `IC-5`, an `IL` row the EL stage does not read (`EC-2` and `EC-3` are byte-identical across all three runs, asserted field by field). (2) The replay goldens pin **recorded** answers, not **reproducible** ones; a green golden proves the pipeline processes a given set of answers correctly and does not prove a fresh run would obtain them. Nothing in the repository claimed otherwise — `docs/llm-evaluation.md` and `02_quality.md` §6.5 both drew the distinction already — but both argued it from a docstring, and this is the first measurement. What *can* change is what the artefacts admit: the provenance block could record that a run's answers are not expected to reproduce, and a re-run comparison tool would let a user measure the spread on their own model rather than assume it. **Recorded as a finding rather than a task**, because the actionable half already shipped: flag-only (F-145) is the response, and this row is its strongest justification. Published in `docs/llm-evaluation.md` § *Local models on this corpus: a direct measurement*, which reports the effect size and what it does not undermine — the deterministic stages reproduced exactly, record for record, across all three runs. | **F-162** | **High** | correctness | **F-157 fixed the body read and left the header read, so `compute_mode` still freezes the GUI thread without bound.** `_read_bounded` is called *inside* the `with urlopen(...)` block — i.e. only after `http.client` has read the status line and every header. Driven through a real Tk mainloop the way `_confirm_run` does (`DEFAULT_TIMEOUT=2.0`, 69 header bytes at 0.30 s): **20.75 s frozen, Tk processed no events for 20.84 s.** **Both** stages are exposed — `plugins/07_il/ui.py:1411` makes the identical call from `_run_clicked`. **(b)** `_read_bounded`'s own budget overruns: the deadline is checked *before* the read, so the last read adds a full socket timeout — measured **5.41 s against a 2.0 s budget** with a pre-header stall. **(c)** `read1` returns `b''` at EOF without checking `Content-Length`, where `read()` raises `IncompleteRead`: a server declaring 1000 bytes and sending a complete 62-byte document then closing now answers `cpu` in 0.02 s where `main` answered `unknown`. | **(a) is the defect F-157 exists to prevent, still present, on the code path F-157's own row calls "the most visible failure this wave could have shipped".** The wave's regression test cannot see it: `tests/test_compute_mode.py` uses `BaseHTTPRequestHandler.end_headers()`, which emits the headers in one write, so the test only ever exercises the body phase.
