The GUI thread is now blocked by the budget rather than by the server, which is the property `_confirm_run` was documented as having and did not have. The orphaned worker is bounded twice over and recorded rather than hidden: the socket keeps its own timeout so it cannot outlive a silent server, and the thread is a daemon so it cannot delay exit; cancelling it properly would mean owning the socket, which is a larger change than this row is worth. **(c)** `_read_bounded` now compares what arrived against a declared `Content-Length` and returns empty when short, restoring the guarantee `read()` gave and `read1` silently dropped — without going back to `read()`, whose reason for being replaced still stands. **(b)** is subsumed: the loop's internal overrun no longer reaches the caller, because the caller's bound is the join and not the loop. **Proven the way it was measured**: `tests/test_view_smoke.py::TestComputeModeDoesNotFreezeTheApplication` stands up a raw-socket server dribbling 60 header lines at 0.30 s — 18 s of work — drives `compute_mode` from inside a **real Tk mainloop** with a 100 ms tick, and asserts both the elapsed time and that no gap between ticks approached it. Reverting the fix turns it red and the run takes **18.99 s against a 2.86 s baseline**. It lives in the real-Tk file deliberately: `tests/test_compute_mode.py` uses `end_headers()`, which emits every header in one write, which is exactly why the wave's own regression test could not see this. **Still open, inherited from F-157:** `_fetch_models` has the same shape one function away, on worker threads rather than the GUI thread. Which row and which of the fourteen checks failed is never stated. | Raised as **D-7** in `07_criteria_parsing.md` §9 and §5, with the message strings quoted verbatim: pass is `messagebox.showinfo("Validation OK", f"All good. Warnings: {n_warn}")`, fail is `messagebox.showerror("Validation failed", f"{n_err} row(s) have errors. Fix them before export.")`. The same section records two structural facts about the same function: **"Validate" is not read-only** — checks 6, 7, 11 and 12 mutate the row — and `_render_rows(with_validation=True)` runs `_validate_row` a second time, so one click applies those mutations twice. The only per-row signal is a Treeview tint. **[measured]** neither `_validate_row` nor `_validate` has any test coverage: grepping `tests/` for either name returns nothing. | **The export gate the GUI requires is the one surface with no test and no output.** A user told *"1 row(s) have errors"* over an eight-row table has to find the row by tint and the check by guesswork, and a user told *"All good"* is told that while warnings stand. It compounds **F-166** and **F-167** rather than causing them — both of those rows validate at E=0 W=0, so a better dialog would not have caught either — which is why this is Medium and files as a reporting defect rather than a correctness one. **Not a re-filing of F-65**, whose remedy names `_validate_row`'s missing stage-operator *cross-check*; this row is about what `_validate` does with the findings it already has. **HO-13-1** and **HO-13-2** are the user-facing halves and are unanswered: whether *"Validation OK"* reads as success with warnings outstanding, and whether the two Treeview tints survive row selection. | Display the collected `errs`/`warns` with their row ids — they are already built and thrown away, so the work is presentation, not detection. Make the pass message conditional on `n_warn`. Separately and worth its own commit: make `_validate` read-only, or state in the row that it mutates, since a validator that rewrites the thing it validates cannot be run twice for the same answer. Cross-ref F-65, F-166, F-167, F-170. | S |
| **F-174** | **Low** | correctness | **Duplicate criterion ids are checked nowhere in the pipeline, and the LLM stages collapse them silently.** `_validate_row` sees one row, `_validate` never compares rows, and no id-uniqueness test exists at any stage. `plugins/06_el/screen.py::run_el_screen` then builds `crit_impacts = {c.id: {...} for c in crits}` — a dict comprehension, so duplicate ids merge — while `llm_results[(a_id, c.id)]` lets the second criterion's verdict overwrite the first's for every record. | Raised as **D-8** in `07_criteria_parsing.md` §9 and §5. **[measured] this session:** the comprehension is at `plugins/06_el/screen.py::run_el_screen` and `llm_results` is written at **three sites** in `plugins/06_el/screen.py::run_el_screen` and three more in `plugins/07_il/screen.py::run_il_screen`, all keyed `(a_id, <criterion id>)` — and the three are three *different* statements, not three copies of one: `llm_results[(a_id, c.id)] = ev`, `llm_results[(a_id, cid)] = ev` under a loop variable, and a dict-literal fail-safe assignment. Whichever fires, a second criterion sharing an id overwrites the first for that record; a grep of `plugins/03_harmoniser/inference.py` and `parser.py` for any id-uniqueness check returns nothing. **The contrast is the point:** the *record* id path is guarded twice — `plugins/06_el/screen.py` drops duplicate `local_id` and, since **F-55**, `plugins/_common/parser.py::_parse_csv_tolerant_text` records them as `duplicate_local_id:<id>` in the audit trail. | Latent on every artefact in this repository — the eight-criterion reference table has no duplicate, and a duplicate id can only arrive through a hand-edited or externally-produced criteria table, which `plugins/03_harmoniser/parser.py::_load_structured_criteria_table` accepts. When it does arrive, one criterion is silently not applied and the counts report as if it were. Low because it is unreachable through the documented workflow and because the direction of harm is under-application. **Not a duplicate of F-55 or F-87**, both of which concern duplicate *record* ids: F-55 is `(done)` and F-87's companion route measures a record-id collapse. This is the same shape one level up, on the axis nothing guards. | Reject duplicate ids in `_validate` with the row ids named — it is the one check that needs to compare rows and the natural place for it, alongside **F-173**'s reporting fix. The consumer-side collapse is then unreachable and needs no second guard. Cross-ref F-55, F-87, F-173, F-143. | XS |
| **F-175** | **Low** | provenance | **`plugins/03_harmoniser/parser.py::_get_best_text_targets` collapses to a single field whenever the best-covered one exceeds 0.8, so `llm` criteria about a paper's content record `target = keywords` alone.** The recorded target is then a statement about the run that is not true of the run. | Raised as **D-9** in `07_criteria_parsing.md` §9 and §7.2. **[read]** the policy is explicit in the function: `if float(text_stats.get(sorted_fields[0], 0.0)) > 0.8: return sorted_fields[0]`. **[measured]** the coverage figures the function is fed are `keywords` 1.000, `title` 0.995, `abstract` 0.775 — **over the first 200 rows only, not the corpus**, because `plugins/03_harmoniser/parser.py::_load_a_header_and_stats` takes `sample_n: int = 200` and breaks at `total >= sample_n`. Executed with exactly those inputs, `_get_best_text_targets` returns `'keywords'`, and every `llm` row in `tests/golden/criteria_harmonized_v3.1.0.csv` carries `target = keywords` — `IC-1`, `EC-2` and `EC-3`, whose labels are about the paper's subject matter. **The 200-row sample is worth naming rather than smoothing over**: a decision that fixes the recorded target of every LLM criterion is taken from the first 200 of 776 records, so a corpus whose abstract coverage improves after row 200 would be described by a cell computed before it was seen. | **Behaviourally inert today and that is the whole of the severity argument.** `plugins/06_el/prompt.py::_build_llm_messages_for_criterion` packs `a_id/title/abstract/keywords` regardless of the `target` cell, and `plugins/_common/llm_client.py::FIELD_VOCABULARY` is `("title","abstract","keywords")`, so the model sees all three whatever the cell says. What is wrong is the record: the exported `criteria_harmonized.csv` and the frozen `docs/data/study_input/` copy both assert a target the stage did not honour, and a reader reconstructing the run from the bundle would conclude the model was shown keywords alone. It becomes a behaviour change the moment the target cell is honoured — which **F-65**'s remedy and the EL/IL target-resolution path both move toward. Low, as a provenance inaccuracy rather than a wrong result. | Either record all available text fields when the stage will send all of them, or state in the exporter that the cell is advisory at EL/IL. Cross-ref F-65, F-88, F-155, and **F-95**, which is the neighbour to read beside this one: F-95 is about what the published quote-validity figures actually measured when the model quoted from semicolon-delimited keyword lists, i.e. the *consequence* of everything being pointed at `keywords`; this row is the `> 0.8` collapse that records it. | **F-177** | **High** | reproducibility / testing | **The project's documented JORS-evidence command fails on the machine it is documented for, and reports success while doing it.** `Dockerfile`'s `COPY . .` copies the **working tree**, not the index, so a build launched from a Windows checkout carries CRLF bytes into a Linux container — where `docs/data/eval_summary_v1.txt` is then compared against a container-generated LF file and fails. `docker_test.sh` has `set -e` but no `set -o pipefail`, so the `docker run … \| tee` pipeline exits 0 and the script prints its *"Use this as evidence for the JORS editor"* banner over a red suite. | **[measured] this session by building and running the image**, then removing it. `git check-attr -a` returns `text: auto` for `docs/data/eval_summary_v1.txt` — F-99's rule is deliberately scoped `docs/data/*.csv` and does not reach a `.txt` — so the file is LF in the index (2571 B, `db85950f…`) and CRLF in this Windows working tree (2633 B, `ef639949…`, 62 CR). `tools/eval_ingest.py::write_summary_text` ends `path.write_text(…)` with no `newline=`, i.e. platform-native, while the three sibling CSV writers in the same file all pin `newline=""`. Inside the container: `os.linesep='\n'` and the copied file is still `ef639949…` with CR=62. `docker run --rm <image>` → **`1 failed, 1574 passed, 34 skipped`**, exit 1, the failure being `tests/test_study_input_freeze.py::TestTheFrozenInputReproducesThePublishedResults::test_output_is_byte_identical_to_the_committed_artefact[eval_summary_v1.txt]`, *"At index 62 diff: b'\n' != b'\r'"*. **Counterfactual in the same image**, normalising that one file to LF in an ephemeral layer: **`1575 passed, 34 skipped`** — exactly the figure F-164's cell records from an LF build context. **Precisely one test flips.** The `set -e`/`tee` behaviour was replicated outside the tree: pipeline status 0, replica script exit code 0, over a tee'd file whose last line reads `1 failed`. | **High on "blocks peer review", and it is the peer-review half specifically.** `Dockerfile`'s own header reads *"Evidence for JORS requirement #4: UNIX-based system testing"* and `docker_test.sh` closes *"Use this as evidence for the JORS editor."* The script builds from `.` — the invoker's working tree — and the invoker's working tree is Windows. So the documented evidence command, run where its author runs it, produces a red suite; and because the exit status is swallowed by the pipe, **the terminal says success while the artefact submitted to the editor says `1 failed`.** Not Critical: no scientific output is wrong and nothing is lost — the published artefact is genuinely reproducible byte-for-byte on the platform that wrote it and LF-for-LF in a Linux clone. **This corrects one sentence of F-99 rather than re-filing it.** F-99's fix cell reasons that `eval_summary_v1.txt` is *"byte-identical on every platform only because `::write_summary_text` uses `Path.write_text` with no `newline=`, which is platform-native and so matches whatever the checkout produced"* — every clause confirmed, and true only while the checkout and the writer are the same machine. `COPY . .` splits them. The same reasoning is recorded a second time in `.gitattributes`' own comment block, so the blind spot is in the tree twice. It also qualifies **F-164**'s discharge of **HO-13-9**: that verification is correct, and its parenthetical *"LF build context"* is load-bearing in a way the row does not flag — sound for CI, where `actions/checkout` is always a fresh LF checkout, and unsound for the documented local path. | Two independent one-line changes, and F-99's own warning says why they must land **together**: pinning the file alone inverts the failure onto Windows hosts. Add `docs/data/eval_summary_v1.txt text eol=lf` **and** pin `write_summary_text` to `newline="\n"`, which is what its three siblings in the same file already do — both sides then LF unconditionally, and the platform-native coupling that makes the current state work by coincidence is gone. Separately, add `set -o pipefail` to `docker_test.sh`, so an evidence script cannot exit 0 over a red suite; that half is worth doing whatever is decided about the line endings, and it is the half that made this invisible for the life of the file. **[not established]:** a build from a fresh `core.autocrlf=false` clone was not run end to end — the in-image counterfactual substitutes for it and matches the recorded LF figure exactly. Cross-ref F-99, F-164, F-178 (the same `COPY . .`, a different consequence), F-98, F-165. | XS |
| **F-178** | **High** | security / packaging | **There is no `.dockerignore`, so `COPY . .` copies the entire working tree into the image — including a populated `.env`.** The secret lands in an immutable image layer at `/app/.env`, where deleting the file afterwards does not remove it. The same glob carries roughly 400 MB where about 10 MB is needed, and `docker_test.sh` writes `test_output_linux.txt` into the build context, invalidating the `COPY . .` layer on every run. | **[measured] this session.** `.dockerignore` is absent (`git ls-files`, `ls -la`). `.env` exists in this working tree and is gitignored at `.gitignore:42` — which is precisely why it holds a real key locally; only `.env.example` is tracked. Build-context composition, measured: **two PyInstaller intermediate trees under `build/` at 106 MB each = 212 MB**; **`dist/` 158 MB**, including `metaScreener.exe` and `metaScreener (console).exe` at ~82 MB each — two **Windows PE** binaries shipped into a Linux container whose only job is running pytest; and **`.git` at 16.1 MB of content** (`du` reports 21 MB, which is block allocation over 2,268 mostly tiny objects, not the bytes a build context transfers). All confirmed present in the built image. **Two subtrees under `build/` are excluded from that figure because this session created them** — `build/lib` and an empty `build/bdist.win-amd64`, both residue of the wheel build F-171's cell records making. They are named rather than silently netted out, because a row about a build context contaminated by its own author's build would be exactly the kind of measurement this register does not accept. The whole context sums to **402 MB across 2,798 files** (`du -sh .` reports 408 MB; a separate measurement of the built image put the payload at 386 MB against ~10 MB of files the tests actually need — the three figures differ by measurement method, not by substance). **The `.env` copy is the finding; the size is the aggravation.** | **High, and the escalation condition is stated because it is one step away.** Not Critical today only because **[measured]** there is no publication path: no `docker push`, `docker save` or `docker tag` anywhere in the tree; `.github/workflows/test.yml` is the only workflow and builds no image; and the JORS artefact that gets shared is `test_output_linux.txt`, which is terminal text. **The moment anyone pushes, saves or shares that image this is Critical, and nothing in the repository warns them.** Independently High on the maintenance bar: a roughly **40×** inflation whose payload is actively nonsensical for the target, plus a self-inflicted cache defeat — the `Dockerfile`'s *"Copy requirements first (layer caching)"* comment is defeated one line later by a context that changes on every run, forcing the `pip install -e ".[dev]"` layer to rebuild. **[measured]** `dockerignore` has zero hits across the whole `docs/` tree and all 162 pre-existing register rows, and no row discusses `COPY . .`, image contents or a secret reaching an image. *One qualification the review pass added and it is worth keeping: the phrase "build context" does already occur once in the register, in **F-164**'s parenthetical "LF build context" — which is not a discussion of the build context but a hedge whose load-bearing weight is exactly what **F-177** is about.* **F-48** is the adjacency that needs revisiting rather than merging: it records `dist/` holding two versions' artefacts and concludes *"Gitignored, so no repo impact"* — **gitignored is not dockerignored**, and its Low severity was set on an assumption this row falsifies. | Add a `.dockerignore`. It is one file, carries no behavioural risk, and the minimum useful content is `.env`, `.git`, `build/`, `dist/`, `*.egg-info/`, `__pycache__/`, `.pytest_cache/` and `test_output_linux.txt`. **Do the `.env` line first and separately if anything is deferred** — the rest is size and cache, this one is a credential. Note that the image is *rebuilt* from a fresh context by anyone following `docker_test.sh`, so the fix takes effect immediately with no migration; existing local images already carry whatever they carried, and pruning them is the only remedy for those. Cross-ref F-48 (whose "no repo impact" conclusion this qualifies), F-84, F-177 (same `COPY . .`), F-160 (the other row where a credential reaches somewhere it was not meant to), and **F-47**, which is `(done)` and already records that `docker_test.sh` writes `test_output_linux.txt` into the repository root — the same fact, and its fix cell deliberately left the write in place (*"the output is the JORS UNIX-testing evidence"*). That decision was correct for F-47's consequence, an untracked artefact; this row records a second consequence of the same write that F-47 had no reason to consider, and does not ask for it to be revisited. | XS |
| **F-179** | **Medium** | process | **Wave 13a raised candidate findings against a brief that was never committed, so its candidate series did not survive the session that produced it.** Every prior wave committed its candidates before sweeping them — wave 5's `CF-1..CF-3` in `FIX_WAVE_5_SAMPLES.md`, wave 6's `C-1..C-42`, wave 13's `D-1..D-14` in the two diagnostic documents. Wave 13a is the only wave with no candidate document, so there was nothing for wave 13b to sweep. | **[measured] this session, twice and independently.** An exhaustive search of the working tree, every ref, every commit message and the reflog for `CF-1` … `CF-8` recovers **exactly two**: **CF-4**, named in `91566ed`'s message — *"CF-4: three modules already assert on `.gitattributes` … That is three copies of one pattern, so the brief's 'say so rather than adding a fourth copy' applies. I did not add a fourth."* — and **CF-5**, named in `fac7cfe`'s, whose closing sentence is *"CF-5 stays open."* (*An earlier draft of this cell spliced two clauses of the CF-4 paragraph into a single quotation reading "I did not add a fourth copy"; **[measured]** that string occurs in no commit message on any ref. Corrected by this row's own review pass, and recorded rather than silently fixed, since this row is about evidence that does not survive its session.*) **Six are unrecoverable** and nothing in the artefact record attests that they existed. The only `CF-1`/`CF-2`/`CF-3` in the tree are **wave 5's**, already converted into **F-83**, **F-84** and **F-85** — a pure **name collision**, and treating those rows as wave-13a candidates would corrupt the register. Of the two recovered: **CF-4 is discharged in code** (`91566ed` derived the protected set from the manifests instead of adding a fourth substring assertion, and `11aa4e4` then replaced its hand-written matcher with `git check-attr`), and **CF-5's substance is recorded as an annotation on F-164** — the rule that every documented path installing from `requirements.txt` or PyPI still lacks `httpx`. | **Medium, and the argument is that the convention exists precisely to prevent this.** Not Critical or High: nothing here is wrong output and nothing blocks work today — both recovered items are discharged. This is **F-165's family** — a wave-process step whose output nothing consumes — rather than F-165 itself, which is about CI conclusions. It is *not* **F-131**, which is about the register's counts. **A second, concrete residue is live at HEAD and has no row anywhere:** `06_llm_integration.md` states that Docker *"is the **only** consumer of `requirements.txt`"*, which `fac7cfe` measured false (`README.md` instructs `pip install -r requirements.txt` in three places and `docs/installation.md` in five, including the documented upgrade path) and corrected **in its commit message only**. That correction is annotated onto F-164 by this wave. | Commit the candidate series before the wave that raised it closes, in the wave's own `FIX_WAVE_*.md` or in a diagnostic document — which is what every other wave did without being told. Cross-ref F-147 (which created three of these 28 and explains why they need a real-Tk subprocess at all), F-164, F-177. | XS |

### Totals

*A **derived snapshot**, regenerated from the table above at wave 13b close
(`4a33d45` was the previous regeneration, at wave 13a close). It is not a separate
record: the status of a row is the marker in its **Effort**
cell and nothing else, so these figures can be regenerated and must be regenerated whenever
rows are added or closed. See "How this register is counted" below for the derivation and the
command. Wave 13b confirmed the derivation reproduced the pre-existing block in all 40 cells
**before** applying its delta, as wave 13a did.*

| Severity | Total | Closed | **Open** | — of which unscheduled | scheduled | backlog | parked |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| **Critical** | 8 | 6 | **2** | 2 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| **High** | 53 | 35 | **18** | 18 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| **Medium** | 77 | 29 | **48** | 44 | 2 | 2 | 0 |
| **Low** | 39 | 14 | **25** | 20 | 0 | 3 | 2 |
| **Total** | **177** | **84** | **93** | 84 | 2 | 5 | 2 |

**A snapshot two regenerations back was stale, and the wave when it went stale is
measured rather than asserted: wave 12 session A, not wave 10.** It read
`141 / 69 / 72` — correct when written at wave 11 session C (`8b5a972`), first divergent
at `ef5b0e3` — and was carried unchanged through wave 12 sessions A **and B** while fifteen
rows were added (141 at `8b5a972` to 156 at `6e0bf7b`, which repaired it; session A alone
added nine) — which is **F-131 happening again in the one place F-131 is about**. The
register now runs **F-01..F-180 with the permanent F-56/F-57/F-58 gap, so 177 rows**; the
derivation is at the bottom of this section and takes a few seconds, so the only reason it
drifted is that nobody ran it. Nothing checks it, which is still F-131.

**Wave 13b opens fifteen rows, closes none, and is a register-only session** — no code, test,
golden, sample or user-facing document was touched, and `03_findings.md` is the only file it
modified. It swept the candidate findings three earlier sessions had measured and never
registered: **D-1..D-10** from `07_criteria_parsing.md` §9, **D-11..D-14** from
`ESCALATION_WAVE_13.md` §Q2g, and wave 13a's own close. **Twenty candidates were inventoried
and every one is accounted for**, which is stated as a count because the coordinator's brief
did not have one and declined to guess: the fourteen `D-n`, the five raised at the wave-13a
close, and the `CF` series as one item. The seven buckets are disjoint and sum to twenty.
**Fourteen became rows** — D-1, D-2, D-4..D-12, the docker build-context and `.dockerignore`

was already recorded before this session**: the `docs/installation.md` test path, annotated
onto F-164 by `4a33d45`. **One was repaired in place rather than logged**: F-153's unescaped
pipes, folded into F-131's family in `e864fe3`. *(An earlier draft of this paragraph said
"twenty-three" and split the buckets so that D-14 fell in two of them at once; the arithmetic
summed and the buckets did not partition. Corrected by this wave's review pass.)* **A fifteenth row, F-180, is not one of the twenty**: it came
out of refuting the flaky-real-Tk candidate, one layer below the claim that failed. Three
further annotations (F-109, F-84, F-164) record measurements made while working candidates
rather than candidates themselves, so the register carries **five** annotations from this
session and **eight** pre-existing rows were touched in all, the other three being F-65,
F-131 and F-153 in `e864fe3`. The refutations are
the part worth reading first, because both were proposed as findings and neither survived
execution. **D-14** — *"a stored per-stage endpoint silently overrides `OPENAI_BASE_URL` and
nothing tells the operator"* — rests on the precedence being undocumented in any user-facing

container fails at a fixed point upstream of every nondeterministic path in that file, so the
same image gives the same 34 skips every run. What that second refutation **did** surface,

**Two Criticals open here, and this register has had none open since wave 7.** **F-166** — a

of 776 records removed, `_validate_row` reporting clean — and **F-168**, the published

and pinned by `SHA256SUMS`, so a corrected harmoniser moves no byte there and would instead
put the freeze test and the published numbers into open contradiction. Each row states the
boundary in its own cell, because a register with eight duplicates behind it does not get to
split a defect silently.

**One candidate was declined rather than opened, and the reasoning is the convention's.**
`in_list` is implemented, tested and emitted by no inference branch — recorded inside F-167's
mechanism. The standing rule is that a "consider also" gets its own row before the parent
closes; this one does not, because F-167's remedy **is** to emit `in_list`, so the row would
be closed by the commit that closes F-167, and a row that cannot outlive its parent is the
duplication this register exists to avoid. The two facts that *do* outlive F-167's fix —
`regex` is still unreachable, and `_detect_contradictions_simple` inspects only
`("equals", "in_list")` while ignoring the five operators the engine actually emits — are
annotated onto **F-109**, whose subject is the operator vocabulary's integrity.

### How this register is counted, and which rows are closed

*Four facts that have each cost a verification cycle. Points 1–3 added in wave 6 and
extended in wave 6b; point 4 added in wave 10, which the intro did not say until wave 13b.*

**1. Count rows, not the maximum ID.** The two are not equal and never will be: the
register contains a permanent gap at **F-56, F-57, F-58**. At wave-9 close there were
**140 rows** running F-01..F-143 (`f014e2f`, derived); at wave-12 session B close there
were **160**, running F-01..F-163; at wave-13b close there are **177**, running
F-01..F-180. A count taken from the highest ID overstates by three, always.
*(This point said "141 rows running F-01..F-144" of wave 9 from `6e0bf7b` until wave 13b.
That is the wave-11-session-A state — F-144 was not opened until then, which this same
document says twice elsewhere — so the illustration of a counting rule was itself
miscounted. **F-131.**)*

The gap is not an accident and must not be backfilled. All three IDs were assigned to
real findings and consumed **outside this register**, in commits whose messages open with
the capitalised phrase "NOT IN THE DIAGNOSTIC REGISTER" — so the off-register status was
deliberate and contemporaneous:

| ID | Commit | Subject |
|---|---|---|
| F-56 | `a6d1f0f` | `fix(F-56): re-encode docs_/samples/ex_ref_2.txt from cp1252 to UTF-8` |
| F-57 | `d6af29c` | `fix(F-57): drop two more unbacked claims from docs/usage.md` |
| F-58 | `8a000ac` | `fix(F-58): add the required --criteria flag to eval_ingest's usage example` |

All three are recorded in `CHANGELOG.md` [Unreleased] → ### Documentation, though unevenly:
F-58 has its own clause, F-56 survives as one clause in a three-ID bullet, and **F-57 is a
bare parenthetical with no description** — `d6af29c`'s commit message is the only substantive
record of F-57 in the repository. New IDs continue past the maximum; wave 5 started at F-83
and wave 6 at F-86 for this reason.

*(A note on counting mechanics, since this is where the miscounts came from: rows begin
`| **F-nn**`, with the ID bolded. A pattern matching `^| F-` returns zero.)*

**2. A row's status is the marker in its Effort cell, and nothing else.** The vocabulary is
closed:

| Marker | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `(done)` | Closed. The fix cell names the commit(s). |
| `(scheduled)` | A named future wave owns it. **Two rows carry it: F-79** (wave 4b) and **F-135** (wave 12, with the model re-capture). **F-142** was closed in wave 9; **F-135**'s marker was removed there — because a marker asserting a wave that has already passed is a claim the register cannot support — and *restored* at wave 10 against a wave that had not passed. This cell read *"F-79 … the only one left"* from wave 9 until wave 13b, contradicted by the rows, by the scheduled column of the Totals table (2), and by the Totals narrative's own *"The Medium tier's scheduled column is consequently 2, not 1."* **F-131**, in the one lookup a maintainer consults to resolve a row's status. |
| `(backlog)` | Deliberately not scheduled: **F-77, F-78, F-80, F-81, F-82**. |
| `(parked)` | Logged, but something must happen before it can be fixed: **F-84, F-85**. |
| *no marker* | Open and unscheduled. |

"Open" in the totals means **everything that is not `(done)`** — so it includes the
scheduled, backlog and parked rows, which are broken out separately because they are
decisions rather than oversights. The totals are a **derived snapshot** and must be
regenerated when rows are added or closed; nothing checks them, which is **F-131**. To
regenerate, count the `(done)` markers against the bolded severity of each row beginning
`| **F-nn**` — noting that six severity cells carry a revision, and that in
`**Medium** → **High**` the current severity is the one *after* the arrow. Getting that
wrong is not hypothetical: the count line printed before wave 6b said `20 High · 36 Medium`
where the table said 21 and 35, because it recorded **F-26** at the severity it was filed
at rather than the High it was raised to in wave 2. Wave 6 inherited that error and carried
it forward.

**3. Closure was recorded in three different places, and only the rows are authoritative.**
The "**Fixed in `<sha>`:**" convention began in wave 3 and was applied consistently only
from wave 4; waves 0–2 recorded their closures in `CHANGELOG.md` and never wrote them back.
**Wave 6b annotated every row for which closing evidence could be established from the
repository**, so the Effort marker is now the single authority and this table is history
rather than a lookup. Of the 139 rows *as they stood at wave 8 part 2*, **56 were closed** —
the five most recent then were F-93, F-111, F-112, F-119 and F-137, closed in that part and
annotated in the same wave. (Both figures are of that moment and are not the current count;
the Totals table above is the current one.) *The scope label on those two figures said
**"as they stood at wave 6b"** from `6e0bf7b` until wave 13b, and wave 6b's own figures were
**129 rows, 45 closed** — `3f81082` printed exactly that. A stale number wearing a
correct-looking of-that-moment label is worse than a bare one, because it defeats the check
a careful reader would otherwise make. **F-131.***

**The partial-closure convention, now that it has been used twice.** F-93 was the first row
to carry one: wave 8 part 1 fixed its engine and artefact half in `d82470e` and left the GUI
half undone, so the row kept an **empty Effort marker** and its fix cell said, in the first
sentence, which half was which. Part 2 finished it in `a0e95b0` and the annotation names both
commits. **F-118 carried one** from wave 8 until wave 11 session C: two of its three halves
were fixed in `866c988` and the third was a scheduled product decision, so it kept an empty
marker for three waves. The third half closed in `8a3f188` and the marker is now `(done)`.
The convention did exactly what it exists for — the row was never readable as finished while
a half of it was outstanding, across three waves and two coordinators' decisions about it. The convention that emerged is worth stating, because it cost nothing and caught
something: *the Effort marker stays empty until every half is done, the fix cell opens with
which half was fixed and which was not, and the totals count the row as open.* An Effort cell
reading `(done)` over a half-fixed row is the failure mode this whole section exists to
prevent, and the same reasoning kept F-133 out of F-104's closure in wave 7.

| Where the closure was recorded before wave 6b | Rows | Count |
|---|---|---|
| **In the row** | F-09, F-23, F-24, F-40, F-54, F-61, F-67–F-76, F-83 | 17 |
| **In `CHANGELOG.md` [Unreleased] only** — annotated in wave 6b | F-01–F-08, F-10, F-13, F-16, F-17, F-18, F-26, F-29, F-30, F-31, F-34, F-37, F-38, F-41, F-42, F-45, F-46, F-50, F-51, F-55 | 28 |
| **Nowhere at all** — closed in a commit, in neither the register nor the changelog | **F-47** | 1 |

**4. Closing a row that changed user-visible behaviour also means writing
`CHANGELOG.md`.** Added at wave 10 as the second half of **F-141**, whose first half was
three missing disclosures. The register and the wave briefs live under `docs/internal/`;
the changelog is the only place this project speaks to someone who has already produced
results. Waves 0–2 wrote the changelog and never wrote the rows, and wave 6b spent a pass
repairing that; waves 7–9 wrote the rows and never wrote the changelog, and wave 10 spent a
pass repairing that. **The convention has now failed in both directions, which is why it is
recorded here rather than in a wave brief** — F-141 exists precisely because the practice
depended on whether a brief happened to name it. The test is not "was a finding closed" but
"could a past user's results differ": if yes, the closure is not complete until the
`[Unreleased]` section says so, in the *"If you produced results with an earlier version"*
list when the defect could change which records were included or excluded, and under
`### Fixed` otherwise. Cross-ref F-131 — the same hand-maintained-bookkeeping shape, one
document over.

**The method that built the first version of this ledger was unsound, and the failure is
worth keeping.** It derived "closed" as *(cited in `CHANGELOG.md`) ∪ (annotated in-row)* and
treated the remainder as open. Both inputs are human bookkeeping; the commit log is the only
mechanically-generated record. **F-47** — fixed in `30ceb3f`, recorded in neither — was
therefore invisible, and the bias runs toward small findings, because a one-line `.gitignore`
fix is exactly what feels too trivial to changelog. The obvious repair, grepping commit
subjects for `F-nn`, fails in the opposite direction and would have been worse: it would
have closed **F-27** and **F-66**, both of which have commits naming them in the subject line
and both of whose defects are fully present at HEAD. A commit tag is a pointer to a finding,
not a claim of closure. The only method that settles a row is to read its stated fix and
check it against HEAD, which is what wave 6b did; `F-11`, `F-27`, `F-43` and `F-66` are the
four rows where that check overturned the ledger, and each says so in its own fix cell.

**F-01..F-05 remain the precedent worth naming** for closure-recorded-outside-the-register,
and they are still not the same class as F-56..F-58: F-01..F-05 had rows whose *closure* was
recorded elsewhere, F-56..F-58 have no rows at all. The first was a stale row, repairable by
annotation, and wave 6b repaired it. The second is an absent row, and the wave-5 decision not
to backfill it stands.

One consequence a reader should still carry: `§ Top 10 actions` below lists F-10 as action 1,
F-01 as action 2, and F-02 and F-03 as action 4, and **all four are closed** — that section is
a record of the original prioritisation, not a work queue. *This sentence said "actions 1, 2,
4 and 5" from `4fe5f0c` until wave 13b; action 5 names F-05, F-16, F-17, F-29, F-30 and F-37
and has never named any of the four. The Top 10 table is byte-identical to the one this
sentence was written against, so this one was wrong at birth rather than stale — the only
member of the set that a re-derivation would have caught on the day.*

### Note on F-64: what was reported, and what was actually there

F-64 was raised as "`{el,il}_evidence_json` reports `UNCERTAIN` without recording
why — the `valid_quote=false` reason is visible only in the cache, which does not
survive into the exported bundle."

**The premise is wrong in the direction of pessimism.** `quote_valid` *is*
exported, alongside `confidence`, `threshold`, `decision` and `used`
(`06_el/screen.py:630-640`). Every exported LLM evidence entry carries nine keys.
An auditor holding only the bundle can already separate the causes, and on the
committed IL golden this resolves cleanly: 30 `UNCERTAIN` below threshold, 7 with
a failed quote, 0 unexplained.

So the audit the finding asked for is possible today, and the archived
2026-05-07 run's 38 `EC-3` uncertains are marked `quote_valid: false` in its own
`EL_FULL.csv` — no cache needed.

What the check did surface is larger than what was alleged: **84** IL entries, not
7, carry no diagnostic keys at all, because the non-llm branch emits a different
shape. That is the defect recorded above. It is worth noting that the alleged
gap and the real one point at the same fix — one shape, one explicit reason
field — which is why this is logged as F-64 rather than dismissed.

### Note on F-61: a suspected Critical that did not survive checking

F-61 was opened on the hypothesis that a criterion switched off in the UI was
still being evaluated and could still exclude records — a visible control that
silently does nothing, which would have been Critical and would have meant past
users needed to re-check their exclusions.

**That hypothesis is false, and nothing in it should be carried into release
notes or user-facing documentation.** Three independent filters stand between
the toggle and an evaluation loop, and the first two predate wave 0:

1. Plugin 03's table writes `enabled=0` into `criteria_harmonized.csv`
   (`03_harmoniser/ui.py:695-699` → `exporters.py:87`).
2. The EH/IH loader skips disabled rows when building the report, so they never
   enter `CriteriaLoadReport.criteria` at all (`_common/parser.py:390-392`).
3. EL/IL filter again at run (`06_el/screen.py:410`, `07_il/screen.py:412`).

The runner's missing filter sat *behind* filter 2 and was therefore never
reached in the application. It was found only because a test constructs a
`CriteriaLoadReport` directly, bypassing the loader.

Recorded at Low rather than dropped because the code fact is real and the fix
is committed, and because the negative result is worth keeping: the next person
to notice `crits = criteria_report.criteria` should be able to find out in one
place that it was checked and found harmless.

The claim also appeared, wrongly, in the commit message of `dc622fa` and in the
first draft of the `[Unreleased]` changelog entry for F-34. The changelog was
corrected before release; the commit message stands as written and is wrong on
this point. This paragraph is the correction of record.

---

### Note on F-73 / F-23: one defect, logged twice, closed once

F-73 (from the report-production diagnostic) and the pre-existing F-23 are
the same defect — EL/IL's single-attempt `errors="replace"` decode versus
EH/IH's four-encoding ladder. The diagnostic re-derived it from source
without recognising the earlier row. Both are closed by `b2fa7df`. One fact
in the duplication is worth keeping: F-23 predicted the fix would change
EL/IL output bytes and require a golden re-capture; measured in wave 4a, it
does not — the ladder's first attempt is byte-identical for UTF-8 input,
which every golden is, and the suite passed with no golden movement.

### Note on F-72 / F-24: one defect, logged twice, closed once

F-72 (from the report-production diagnostic) and the pre-existing F-24 are
the same defect — EL/IL padding or truncating ragged corpus rows that EH/IH
reject as `bad_column_count`. The diagnostic re-derived it from source
without recognising the earlier row. Both are closed by `58518c9`. The
second logging was not pure duplication: F-24 recorded only the policy
divergence ("different record sets depending on which stage happens to
run"), while F-72 added the audit-trail half — the repair left no entry in
what becomes `data/input_errors.csv`, so a silently corrected record was
invisible — and it is that consequence the fix's tests pin. A duplication
sweep of the remaining wave-4 rows (F-67..F-82 against F-01..F-66) found no
further pair: the F-68→F-03, F-74→F-03 and F-82→F-53 relationships are
residuals of or adjacencies to already-closed or distinct findings, and are
cross-referenced inside those rows.

### Note on F-74: the EH/IH and EL/IL export buttons are not symmetrical

After F-71 and F-74, the two "Export input_errors.csv…" buttons export
different things, deliberately. **EH/IH export the accumulated trail** —
the bundle's incoming input_errors.csv carried verbatim plus this stage's
own drops (`plugins/04_eh/ui.py:903-904` and the IH twin, via
`_export_input_errors_csv(..., prior_text=...)`,
`plugins/_common/exporters.py:49`). **EL/IL export this stage's own skips
only** (`plugins/06_el/ui.py:987-988`, `plugins/07_il/ui.py:1238-1239`, via
`_export_input_errors_csv_from_dicts`, which takes no prior text). The
reason is structural: EL/IL's `_load_bundle` never reads the bundle's prior
input_errors.csv, so their views have no accumulated trail to offer —
adding that read was outside wave 4a's scope. Both write the canonical
six-column schema, and the *bundle's* input_errors.csv is the accumulated
record at every stage regardless; only the standalone button files differ.
No code change in this wave; noted for a future alignment pass.

---

### Note on F-27: the mechanism, supplied by the report-production diagnostic

F-27 recorded that the manifest carries two divergent stage maps without saying
which writer causes the divergence. `05_report_production.md` candidate 13 and
§A.0 supply it: **EH/IH update only `pipeline.stages`**
(`plugins/_common/bundle.py:401-403`), while **EL/IL update both**
(`plugins/_common/bundle.py:551-556`).

Observed on a bundle driven through all four stages: `pipeline.stages` reads
`{EH: done, IH: done, EL: done, IL: done}` while `pipeline_state.stages` still
reads `{EH: not_run, IH: not_run, EL: done, IL: done}`. So the divergence is not
symmetric drift — it is two stages writing one map and two stages writing both,
against a Harmoniser that creates only the second
(`plugins/03_harmoniser/exporters.py:225-228`).

That narrows the fix: whichever key is chosen, the change needed is in
`_export_next_bundle_zip` alone. No renumbering; F-27's severity and status are
unchanged.

---

### Note on F-14: the third `DataTable`, and the record of why

F-49 recorded that `_common/widgets.py` claims to serve EL/IL but does not.
`05_report_production.md` candidate 15 and §C.1 complete the count: there are
**three** copies of the class — `plugins/_common/widgets.py:38`,
`plugins/06_el/ui.py:82`, `plugins/07_il/ui.py:89` — and the **render path is
character-identical across all three**. The EL/IL copies differ only in a legacy
positional-columns constructor, two helpers (`set_rows`, `get_selected_row`) and
`RENDER_CHUNK` (400 vs 300).

`plugins/_common/widgets.py:22-23` records the exclusion in the module's own
docstring: *"When EL and IL undergo their own decomposition, they will import the
same DataTable rather than carrying private copies."* That decomposition happened
in Conv 6 and the widget did not move with it.

Relevant to F-14's migration order: the row-preparation logic was unit-tested
headlessly against hostile metadata during this diagnostic and found clean in all
three copies, so adopting the shared widget carries no behavioural risk beyond
the constructor signature. No renumbering; F-14 and F-49 are unchanged.

### Wave-6 sweep: `06_llm_integration.md` §B9 against F-01..F-85

**What was swept against what.** The 42 candidates of the single deduplicated `C-n`
namespace in `06_llm_integration.md` § "B9 Candidate findings" (committed `895e51c`,
2026-08-09) against **all 82 rows** then in this register — not only against the rows
the diagnostic named inline. Swept on **2026-08-09**, at `895e51c`, on branch
`fix/wave-6-register`. The diagnostic deliberately assigned no `F-nn` and did not touch
this file; §B9's own note said the sweep was "a later wave". This is that wave.

**Outcome.** 38 candidates became **45 new rows, F-86..F-130**; four were true duplicates
or refinements of existing rows and were merged rather than added. No row was deleted and
nothing was renumbered.

**The true duplicates — four, and only four.**

- **C-16 → F-12.** F-12's *substance* is still true, but its headline ("the entire LLM
  interaction path is 0% covered … never executed") is stale:
  `tests/test_cancellation.py` drives `run_m1_llm_for_criterion` directly against a fake
  client. F-12 was **narrowed**, not closed. Its evidence cell's *mechanism* was also
  wrong and is corrected in place — the golden tests do not short-circuit because
  `test_el_regression.py` unsets the key (it does unset it), but because a complete cache
  leaves `to_call` at zero, so the guard in `plugins/06_el/screen.py::run_el_screen` is
  never entered.
- **C-21 → F-25.** A magnitude sharpening, as the diagnostic offered it: 3 × 600 s, not
  600 s, because the SDK also defaults `max_retries=2`. It carried one thing beyond
  magnitude — every logged "batch failed" line under-reports the request count threefold —
  which is folded into the same row because the same fix closes it. F-25's `max_tokens` /
  `finish_reason` half is preserved; a merge that replaced the row would have deleted it.
- **C-22 → F-63 and F-64.** The only candidate that split across two existing rows. Its
  span half is a straight duplicate of **F-63** and contributes new evidence (169/170 EL
  and 77/84 IL golden spans do not locate their quote — so the defect is in the shipped
  fixtures, not merely in one archived run). Its `error` half extends **F-64**, whose
  proposed `uncertain_reason` vocabulary has no member for "the call failed".
- **C-23 → F-22.** A refinement: the gate normalises whitespace only, and F-22's proposed
  NFKC fix is necessary but not sufficient — measured, NFKC leaves U+200B/200C/200D,
  U+FEFF and U+00AD untouched and does not fold case.

**Near-misses checked and rejected**, so the next reader does not re-derive them: C-2
against F-33 (same artefact, opposite direction — F-33 is unreported corruption on *read*,
C-2 is deliberate persistence of failures on *write*); C-4 against F-01 (same shape,
different axis, and F-01 is closed); C-6 against F-08 (C-6 is F-08's *residual*, not a
duplicate — F-08 removed the key rule and left the endpoint barrier standing, which is why
F-91 records that F-08's changelog entry over-states its own closure); C-13 against F-28
(both concern the goldens; F-28 is about the settings they were captured at, C-13 about
the two incompatible roles they serve); C-19 against F-33; C-26 against F-69 (the same
shape with the model as consumer, but F-69 is closed and its fix does not reach a prompt);
C-31 against F-81 (same files, different defect); C-35 against F-15 and F-09/F-40; C-36
against F-16 and F-83; C-37 against F-25; C-41 against F-15; C-42 against F-49, F-50 and
F-51.

**Corrections to the sweep brief's own premises**, recorded because they were checked
rather than accepted: C-23's claim that F-22 "implies `_normalize_space` mishandles the
NBSP class" is **withdrawn** — F-22's row never mentions NBSP, whitespace classes or
spaces, and says only that the check is "not Unicode-normalised or case-folded", naming
NFC/NFD and smart punctuation. The *code* fact behind the claim is true and is recorded in
F-22's refinement; the attribution to F-22 was not, and F-22 needed no correction on that
point. Likewise C-24 was offered as "F-66's failure shape one layer down": only its
spec-test half is, and the candidate was split (F-105, F-106) rather than merged.

Ordered by (impact × urgency) ÷ effort, with peer review in mind.

The "local pre-commit gate only" policy has now failed once in production; CI is the only durable fix. |
| **2** | **Fix the criterion-content cache key (F-01).** Hash `crit_pack` into `_cache_key`, re-capture the EL/IL goldens, and note the re-capture in the CHANGELOG. | S + re-capture | Removes the one path where the reproducibility mechanism actively produces wrong science. This is the finding that most deserves to be closed before publication. |

## What is genuinely good here

This is a better-instrumented project than its size and its one-maintainer history would
predict. The following should be treated as load-bearing and **not** disturbed by any
refactor:

- **The `PASS_FLAGGED` discipline is real and consistently applied.** Almost every failure
  path inside the screening engines — unparseable JSON, missing column, empty field,
  unrecognised operator, API outage, low confidence, unverifiable quote — routes to *human
  review*, never to exclusion. `llm_client.py:290-303` and `:357-370` explicitly back-fill an
  uncertain entry for every item in a batch that produced no response, so a failed API call
  cannot make a record disappear. That is the correct instinct for a screening tool and it is
  applied uniformly.

- **The evidence gate holds up under adversarial reading.** I traced every field of the LLM
  response looking for a way past it: invented `a_id`, out-of-enum decision, non-numeric or
  out-of-range confidence, bogus field name, malformed span, non-JSON response, prose-wrapped
  response. Every one degrades to "flagged", not to "acted on". The one hole (F-21, trivial
  quotes) is narrow and closable in one line. Crucially, the quote is validated against **the
  exact truncated text the model was shown**, recomputed per call because adaptive retry can
  change the truncation mid-batch (`llm_client.py:273-277`) — that is a subtle thing to get
  right and it is right.

- **The golden-file mechanism is the best thing in the repository.** Five byte-identity
  goldens covering all four screening stages plus the criteria table, replayed offline
  through a cached-response envelope so an LLM-dependent stage becomes deterministically
  testable with zero network and zero cost — and engineered so that a *cache miss* flips the
  outcome column and fails loudly rather than silently passing. The `.gitattributes`
  `binary` rule protecting them from CRLF rewriting is load-bearing and correct (the goldens
  genuinely mix LF and CRLF). This machinery is what makes the Phase 4 de-duplication
  tractable at all.

- **The two AST audit tools are an unusual and genuinely good idea.** `tools/audit_imports.py`
  exists because a real bug — a transitive import dropped during the Plugin 03 extraction —
  survived a green test suite and was only caught by manual GUI smoke. Rather than shrug, the
  author wrote a static checker for that exact bug class, documented the incident in the
  tool's docstring, and wired it into CI. Both audits pass clean today.

- **The validation study is done properly.** Blind adjudication grids with the LLM's columns
  deliberately stripped, and a test (`test_decisions_sheets_do_not_expose_llm_columns`) that
  enforces the blindness. Polarity-aware mapping so humans and LLM are compared on one
  canonical scale. Pure-Python Cohen's and Fleiss' kappa — **which I verified independently
  against textbook reference values and they are exactly correct**, including the edge cases
  (empty input, perfect agreement, single category / zero marginals → NaN rather than a
  division by zero, unequal rater counts → `ValueError`). The full evidence chain is
  committed under `docs/data/`, and the reported kappas include *negative* values that the
  documentation then explains as the prevalence paradox rather than hiding.

- **The refactoring history is disciplined.** Twenty-odd commits titled
  `refactor(plugin-NN): extract X`, each small, each preceded by the golden capture that
  protects it (`4977cf0` and `4fbe8fd` land *before* the extractions they guard). Two
  follow-up commits (`90ff050`, `d277a33`) fix defects the author found in their own earlier
  extractions. `_common/` for EH/IH is a complete and successful de-duplication — the residual
  difference is 12 lines of `plugin.py` plus 20 lines of strings.

- **Honesty in the documentation where it counts.** `llm_client.py:150-155` states plainly
  that temperature 0.0 does not guarantee determinism and that the cache is the real
  reproducibility mechanism. The CHANGELOG's **Deferred** section names the exact obstacle to
  further de-duplication and the exact reviewer items that were not addressed, rather than
  quietly omitting them. Plugin 01 is labelled experimental in the README, in `docs_/README.md`,
  in the tab title, in a banner widget, and in its docstring — five places, with an explicit
  warning that PRISMA flow diagrams are not valid input.

- **Zero bare `except:` clauses in 22,175 lines.** The 183 broad handlers are a problem, but
  every one of them names `Exception`.

- **The headline funnel numbers check out.** 776 records confirmed by parsing the sample
  corpus; 90.6% and 98.3% are arithmetically exact and mutually consistent; the 0.6 default
  threshold is verified in three code locations plus the committed criteria golden. (The final
  count is the one open item — see Q1.)

- **`git status` is clean, no secret has ever been committed** (`git log --all -- .env`
  returns nothing), all seven `.gitignore` sections are thoughtful, and the CI matrix covers
  four operating systems and four Python versions with `fail-fast: false`.

---

## Open questions for the human

**Q1 — The 73-vs-80 discrepancy (highest priority).** `README.md:25` reports the funnel
ending at **73** records. Replaying the committed goldens gives **80** (`il_filtered`: 84 in,
4 `OUT`, 80 `REVIEW`). The 90.6% and 98.3% figures are consistent with 73, not 80. The
goldens were captured at `TRUNC_CHARS=4000, BATCH_SIZE=5`, which are not the application
defaults — so the golden run and the manuscript's demonstration run may legitimately be
different executions. **Which run do the manuscript figures describe, and is that run's
output archived anywhere?** If they are different runs, the repository should say so; if they
are the same run, one of the two numbers is wrong.

**Q2 — Has a PyInstaller build ever been produced and launched from this tree?** F-09 predicts
that `pandas`, `openpyxl`, `langdetect`, `rapidfuzz`, `PIL`, and `pytesseract` are absent from
the frozen bundle, which would make Plugins 01/02/03/04/05 fail silently. I could not test
this. If a working `.exe` exists, my analysis of the spec is missing something and I would
want to know what.

**Q3 — Is the custom plugin loader solving a problem I cannot see?** I demonstrated that stock
`importlib` loads all ten plugin modules in the source tree, and that `_ensure_metascreener_on_sys_path`
already puts `sys._MEIPASS` on `sys.path` for the frozen case. **Was the loader written in
response to a specific PyInstaller failure?** If so, what was the error? That would change the
F-19 verdict from "replace" to "keep, with tests".

**Q4 — Is "Screen A" a deliberate concept or a legacy name?** It appears in tab titles, env
vars, the bundle root prefix, and the final report filename. If it names a *phase* (title/
abstract screening, as opposed to a hypothetical "Screen B" full-text phase), it should be
documented as such. If it is just the old product name, F-36 applies. This affects the bundle
root string, so it is not a cosmetic decision.

**Q5 — What is the intended relationship between EH/IH `PASS_FLAGGED` and IL's `REVIEW`?**
They are the same concept under two labels (`06_el/screen.py:66` vs `07_il/screen.py:68`).
Is the distinction meaningful to a user, or is it an artefact of the twinning?

**Q6 — Should `docs/internal/` exist at all?** F-29 means it currently breaks CI. If internal
documents are wanted in-repo (this report, the reviewer-response map), the tests need an
exclusion; if not, they should live elsewhere and `CHANGELOG.md:35` should be corrected.

**Q7 — What was `docs/internal/reviewer-response-map.md` and where is it now?** The CHANGELOG
announces it as a 3.1.0 deliverable. It is in neither the tree nor any commit.

**Q8 — Is Plugin 02's zero test coverage a deliberate scoping decision?** It is the second-
largest subsystem and every corpus passes through it. Was it treated as out of scope because
it makes network calls, or did it simply never get tests?

**Q9 — How much does the pipeline get run against non-UTF-8 corpora in practice?** Several
findings (F-13, F-23, F-38) concern encoding fallbacks that fail either silently or closed. If
real-world inputs are always UTF-8 exports, these drop in priority; if researchers routinely
paste from Endnote/Zotero on Windows, F-13 in particular becomes urgent.

**Q10 — Is the criteria file expected to change between runs of the same review?** This
determines how severe F-01 really is. If the workflow is "harmonise once, then run stages
04→07 in one sitting", the stale-cache window is small. If criteria get refined after seeing
EL output — which is the realistic research workflow — F-01 fires on the most common path.

**Q11 — Are the EL and IL prompts intended to diverge?** Commit `edd466d` deliberately split
one shared prompt builder into two byte-identical copies "so EL and IL prompts can evolve
independently". Is there a concrete plan for that divergence? If not, F-14's first migration
step is free; if yes, the `StageSpec` design should carry a per-stage prompt hook instead.

**Q12 — Who is the copyright holder?** `LICENSE:3` says `Copyright (c) 2026` with no name;
the SPDX headers say Alejandro Reyes-Consuelo; the affiliations point at Université Laval.
Universities often assert institutional copyright. Worth resolving before deposit.

**Q13 — Was commit `365325c` made through a PowerShell script or an editor?** Identifying the
exact tool would let you fix the local workflow, not just the file. The signature is a
read-as-cp1252 / write-as-UTF-8-with-BOM round trip, which is the PowerShell 5.1 default for
`Set-Content`/`Out-File`.

**Q14 — Does the GUI block export after a cancelled run?** I could not verify F-02's full
severity without running the app. If `EHView`'s export buttons are disabled after a cancel,
the finding drops from Critical to Medium. The engine-level truncation is real either way.

**Q15 — Is there an upper bound on corpus size anyone has tested?** `run_screen` holds the
entire corpus in memory as list-of-dicts and the LLM stages build several parallel dicts
keyed by `(a_id, cid)`. 776 records is comfortable; 50,000 may not be. No finding is raised
because no limit is documented or claimed — but reviewers of screening tools often ask.

---

*See [`README.md`](README.md) for the executive summary.*
